\documentclass[10pt,a4paper]{article}
% A single-file reader generated from the common annotated page master.
% Compile with LuaLaTeX twice. Source IDs and archive links are retained.
\usepackage{fontspec}
\setmainfont{Latin Modern Roman}
\setsansfont{Latin Modern Sans}
\setmonofont{Latin Modern Mono}
\usepackage[english]{babel}
\babelprovide[import]{french}
\usepackage[a4paper,left=21mm,right=43mm,top=22mm,bottom=22mm,
  marginparwidth=29mm,marginparsep=5mm,headheight=14pt,headsep=5mm]{geometry}
\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,mathtools}
\usepackage{newunicodechar}
\newunicodechar{∈}{\ensuremath{\in}}
\newunicodechar{⊗}{\ensuremath{\otimes}}
\newunicodechar{ø}{\ifmmode\text{\normalfont\itshape\char"00F8}\else\char"00F8\fi}
\newunicodechar{Ø}{\ifmmode\text{\normalfont\itshape\char"00D8}\else\char"00D8\fi}
\usepackage{xcolor}
\usepackage{tikz-cd}
\usetikzlibrary{shapes.geometric}
\usepackage{lua-ul}
\usepackage{marginnote}
\usepackage{fancyhdr}
\usepackage{pdflscape}
\usepackage{microtype}
\usepackage[unicode,breaklinks,colorlinks,linkcolor=blue!45!black,
  urlcolor=blue!45!black,pdfencoding=auto]{hyperref}
% Some transcribed symbols deliberately use the slashed-o letter. Select its
% actual text glyph even inside math; do not replace it by another math symbol.
% Install after package encoding declarations have initialized the text macros.
\AtBeginDocument{\RenewDocumentCommand{\o}{}{\ifmmode\text{\normalfont\itshape\char"00F8}\else\char"00F8\fi}}
\AtBeginDocument{\RenewDocumentCommand{\O}{}{\ifmmode\text{\normalfont\itshape\char"00D8}\else\char"00D8\fi}}
% Explicit maps also support minimal TeX Live installations.
\pdfextension mapfile{+cm.map}
\pdfextension mapfile{+symbols.map}
\pdfextension mapfile{+euler.map}
\pdfextension mapfile{+latxfont.map}
\pdfextension mapfile{+cmextra.map}
\pdfextension mapfile{+lm.map}
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt}
\setlength{\parskip}{4pt plus 1pt minus 1pt}
\setlength{\emergencystretch}{1.5em}
\setlength{\marginparpush}{3pt}
\raggedbottom
\definecolor{handblue}{RGB}{25,65,125}
\definecolor{deletedred}{RGB}{132,38,34}
\definecolor{strikegray}{RGB}{90,90,90}
\newcount\EditionMode
\EditionMode=2 % 0 diplomatic; 1 corrected apparatus; 2 reading
\tikzset{handwritten/.code={\ifnum\EditionMode=0
  \pgfkeysalso{/tikz/draw=handblue}\else\pgfkeysalso{/tikz/draw=black}\fi}}
\tikzcdset{handwritten/.style={/tikz/handwritten}}
\newif\ifInApparatus
\newif\ifEditionFrench
\EditionFrenchfalse
\newcommand{\EditionForm}{English reading text}
\newcommand{\CurrentScanURL}{}
\newcommand{\CurrentScanLabel}{}
\newcommand{\CurrentPageKey}{}
\pagestyle{fancy}
\fancyhf{}
\fancyhead[L]{\small\sffamily Nouveau EGA V}
\fancyhead[R]{\small\sffamily\CurrentScanLabel}
\fancyfoot[C]{\small\thepage}
\renewcommand{\headrulewidth}{0.2pt}
\newcommand{\ScanLink}[1]{\href{\CurrentScanURL}{#1}}
\newcount\SourceMarkSerial
\newcommand{\SourceHref}[2]{%
  \global\advance\SourceMarkSerial by 1
  \hypertarget{egamark:#1:\the\SourceMarkSerial}{\ScanLink{#2}}}
\newcommand{\scanpage}[1]{%
  \ifnum\EditionMode=2
    \marginnote{\scriptsize\sffamily\SourceHref{scan}{scan #1}}%
  \else
    \par\medskip\noindent{\small\sffamily\SourceHref{scan}{[scan #1]}}\par\smallskip
  \fi}
\newcommand{\mspage}[1]{%
  \ifnum\EditionMode=2
    \marginnote{\scriptsize\sffamily p.\,#1}%
  \else
    {\small\sffamily[p.\,#1]}%
  \fi}
\NewDocumentCommand{\hand}{+m}{\ifnum\EditionMode=0{\color{handblue}#1}\else#1\fi}
\let\over\relax
\NewDocumentCommand{\over}{+m +m}{%
  {\color{strikegray}#1}\ensuremath{{}^{\nearrow}}{\color{handblue}#2}}
\NewDocumentCommand{\inserted}{O{} +m}{%
  {\color{handblue}\ensuremath{\langle}#2\ensuremath{\rangle}}}
\NewDocumentCommand{\deleted}{+m}{%
  {\color{deletedred}\ifmmode\text{[del.:}\,#1\,\text{]}\else[\textsf{del.:} #1]\fi}}
\NewDocumentCommand{\xout}{+m}{%
  {\color{strikegray}\ifmmode\text{[x:}\,#1\,\text{]}\else[\textsf{x:} #1]\fi}}
\NewDocumentCommand{\uline}{+m}{\ifmmode\underline{#1}\else\underLine{#1}\fi}
\NewDocumentCommand{\uuline}{+m}{\ifmmode\underline{\underline{#1}}\else\underLine[top=-1.05ex,bottom=-1.15ex]{\underLine{#1}}\fi}
\NewDocumentCommand{\unsure}{+m}{\SourceHref{unsure}{[#1?]}}
\newcommand{\illegible}[1]{\SourceHref{illegible}{\ifmmode\text{[illeg. #1]}\else[\textsf{illeg.\ #1}]\fi}}
\newsavebox{\MarginMeasure}
\NewDocumentCommand{\MarginContent}{+m}{%
  \ifInApparatus
    {\small\textsf{\ifEditionFrench marg.\else marg.\fi:}\ #1}%
  \else
    \ifmmode
      \text{\scriptsize[\textsf{marg.:} #1]}%
    \else
      \sbox{\MarginMeasure}{\parbox[t]{\marginparwidth}{\footnotesize\raggedright #1}}%
      \ifdim\dimexpr\ht\MarginMeasure+\dp\MarginMeasure\relax<36pt
        \marginnote{\usebox{\MarginMeasure}}%
      \else
        \par\smallskip\noindent\rule{16mm}{0.3pt}\par\nobreak
        \begingroup\small\noindent\textsf{\ifEditionFrench Note marginale\else Marginal note\fi.}\ #1\par\endgroup
        \nobreak\smallskip\noindent\rule{16mm}{0.3pt}\par\smallskip
      \fi
    \fi
  \fi}
\NewDocumentCommand{\margin}{+m}{{\color{handblue}\MarginContent{#1}}}
\NewDocumentCommand{\placedmargin}{+m}{\MarginContent{#1}}
\NewDocumentCommand{\diagram}{+m}{\par\smallskip{\centering #1\par}\smallskip}
\newenvironment{diagramblock}{\par\begin{center}}{\end{center}\par}
\newcommand{\VariantNumber}[1]{\csname variantnumber@#1\endcsname}
\newcommand{\VariantMark}[1]{%
  \ifInApparatus\else
    \ifmmode\text{\textsuperscript{\tiny\hyperlink{app:#1}{[\VariantNumber{#1}]}}}%
    \else\textsuperscript{\tiny\hyperlink{app:#1}{[\VariantNumber{#1}]}}\fi
  \fi}
\NewDocumentCommand{\var}{+m +m m m}{%
  \ifnum\EditionMode=0#1\else#2\ifnum\EditionMode=1\VariantMark{#4}\fi\fi}
\NewDocumentCommand{\tnote}{m +m}{%
  \ifnum\EditionMode=1\footnote{\textsf{Translator's note (#1).} #2}\fi}
\NewDocumentCommand{\restoredfrom}{m m +m}{%
  \begingroup\def\CurrentScanURL{#2}%
  \ifInApparatus
    {\small\sffamily\SourceHref{provenance}{[scan #1]}}\par
  \else
    \marginnote{\scriptsize\sffamily\SourceHref{provenance}{scan #1}}%
  \fi
  #3\par\endgroup}
\NewDocumentCommand{\AppEntry}{m m m +m +m +m}{%
  \par\smallskip\noindent\hypertarget{app:#1}{\textbf{[#2]}}\quad
  \textit{#3}. #4\par
  \begingroup\InApparatustrue\small
  \ifEditionFrench\textsf{Page :}\else\textsf{Page:}\fi\ 
  {\EditionMode=0 #5}\par
  \ifEditionFrench\textsf{Texte retenu :}\else\textsf{Retained text:}\fi\ 
  {\EditionMode=2 #6}\par\endgroup}
\newcommand{\AbsentReading}{\ifEditionFrench[omis]\else[omitted]\fi}
\newcommand{\WordSpaceReading}{\ifEditionFrench[espace entre les mots]\else[word space]\fi}
\NewDocumentCommand{\RegisterPage}{m +m +m}{%
  \expandafter\long\expandafter\gdef\csname PageBody@#1\endcsname{#2}%
  \expandafter\long\expandafter\gdef\csname PageApparatus@#1\endcsname{#3}}
\newcommand{\PrintPage}[3]{%
  \clearpage
  \gdef\CurrentPageKey{#1}\gdef\CurrentScanLabel{#2}\gdef\CurrentScanURL{#3}%
  \typeout{EGA-SOURCE-BEGIN #1 PDF-PAGE \thepage}%
  \pdfbookmark[1]{Scan #2}{source:#1}%
  \csname PageBody@#1\endcsname\par
  \ifnum\EditionMode=1
    \csname PageApparatus@#1\endcsname
  \fi
  \typeout{EGA-SOURCE-END #1 PDF-PAGE \thepage}}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_002}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 2 (PDF page 3).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/2}
\var{\margin{\unsure{new EGA} $\overline{\underline{V}}$}}{\placedmargin{\unsure{new EGA} $\overline{\underline{V}}$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_002-v0001} \mspage{\hand{0}}

EGA IV 20 \quad \uline{Hyperplane sections and conical projections.} (State \unsure{000|0})

1. Notation.

2. Generic hyperplane section: local properties.

3. Generic hyperplane section: \unsure{geometric} irreducibility and connectedness.

4. Variable hyperplane section: ``sufficiently \unsure{general}'' sections.

5. Theorems of Seidenberg \unsure{type}.

6. Connection of an arbitrary hyperplane section.\tnote{25-002-1}{The source heading uses connexion here, whereas item 3 uses connexité. The supplied glossary renders these words as connection and connectedness, respectively.}

7. Application to constructions of special hyperplane sections and multisections.

8. Dimension of the set of exceptional hyperplanes.

9. Change of projective immersion.

10. Pencils of hyperplane sections, and fibrations of blown-up varieties

11. Grassmannians.

12. Generalization of earlier results to linear sections.

13. Elementary morphisms and M. Artin's theorem.

14. Conical projections.

15. Axiomatization of certain of the preceding results.

16. Translations into the language of linear systems.

\uline{Comments}. This draft gives, in a jumble, a detailed outline
of all the results that should appear in a final
version. To reach that version, the present State 0 will have to be reorganized
from top to bottom, the first task probably being
to draw up a new plan (in which, no doubt, the present
nos. 11,12,14,15 will come much earlier). I have not yet \unsure{written}
no. 16, which in principle should present no difficulty or
affect the preceding nos. in any way, since it is \unsure{a}
\tnote{25-002-2}{Several doubt marks concern damaged endings or overtyped letters rather than a choice between distinct meanings. The French candidates in item 4 are générales and éńérales; in item 5, type and ttype. The final paragraph has the clipped form rédi followed by a doubtful hyphen, and d'u followed by a doubtful n. The corresponding English words remain marked as uncertain. The deleted extra m in item 8 is retained as a letter in the apparatus.}

\var{\margin{2}}{\placedmargin{2}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_002-v0003}

}{%
\par\medskip\noindent\rule{\linewidth}{0.3pt}\par
{\small\sffamily\bfseries Apparatus for this source page}\par

}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_003}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 3 (PDF page 4).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/3}
\var{\margin{\unsure{0bis|06}}}{\placedmargin{\unsure{0bis|06}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_003-v0001}

simple matter of translation.

You will notice the presence of a proposition 10.3. that ought to
appear in an earlier paragraph. In this connection, I point out that I have
several other results, rather disparate but all relating to
birational maps, that I would like to insert somewhere.
It seems to me that there will be too few of them to make a paragraph; \unsure{would}
you have a suggestion as to where to put it? I think I shall send it to you
soon, together with no.16 of these notes.
\tnote{25-003-1}{The French question begins with the clipped form au followed by the doubtful reading rai. Its question word is therefore also marked in English. The familiar verb fourrer, here and below, is rendered as put.}

Besides, the present par.20 will very probably split\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_003-v0002} into
two, one consisting of developments of an ``elementary geometry''
kind on Grassma\unsure{nni}ans. At a pinch, one could also put there
(for lack of a better place) the additions I mentioned on
birational maps?

\mspage{\hand{3}}

}{%

IV 20 \quad \uline{Hyperplane sections and \var{\deleted{linear}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_004-v0001}\var{\inserted{conical}}{conical}{insertion}{ega25_25_004-v0002} projections}.

20.1. \quad \uline{Preliminaries \var{\over{of}{and}}{and}{writing over}{ega25_25_004-v0003} notation}.

Let S be a \quad prescheme, $\underline{E}$ a loc free Module of finite type on S,
$\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}$ its dual. We shall denote by P=$\underline{P}(\underline{E})$ the projective bundle defined by $\underline{E}$,
and by $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ the one defined by $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}$. We shall \unsure{call} \ $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ \ the \uline{scheme of hyperplanes}
of P; this terminology is justified as follows. \ Let
$\hand{\xi}$ be a section of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ over S, which is thus\hand{/}determined by an invertible
quotient module L of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}$. It gives an invertible quotient module
$\underline{L}_P$ of$(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}})_P$ = $(\underline{E}_P)^{\hand{\vee}}$; on the other hand, we have an invertible quotient module
$\underline{O}_P(1)$ of \ $\underline{E}_P$. Passing \ to the duals, we may regard \ $\underline{L}_P^{-1}$ resp. $\underline{O}_P(-1)$
as invertible submodules (loc direct factors) of \ $\underline{E}_P$ resp.
of $(\underline{E}_P)^{\hand{\vee}}$, \ and the pairing \ $\underline{E}_P \otimes \overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}_P \hand{\longrightarrow} \underline{O}_P$ \ therefore defines a
natural pairing
(x) \[ \underline{O}_P(-1)\otimes\underline{L}_P^{-1} \hand{\to} \underline{O}_P \]
or again a transpose \ homomorphism
\var{\margin{$\|$}}{\placedmargin{$\|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_004-v0004}
(xx) \[ \underline{O}_P \hand{\longrightarrow} \underline{O}_P(1)\otimes\underline{L}_P \ = \ \underline{D}_P(1) \ , \]
i.e. a section of $\underline{L}_P(1)$, canonically determined by $\hand{\xi}$. The ``divisor''
of this section, \ i.e. the \var{\inserted{closed}}{closed}{insertion}{ega25_25_004-v0005} subscheme $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ of \ P defined by the Ideal
that is the image of (x), \ is called the \uline{hyperplane in P} \ defined by \ the element
$\hand{\xi} \hand{\in} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}(S)$. \ It can also be described by observing that locally on S \var{\xout{the}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_004-v0006}
\var{\xout{\unsure{datum of $\check{\xi}$ is equivalent}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_004-v0007} \quad $\hand{\xi}$ \ is given by a section \ $\phi$ of \ $\underline{E}$, \var{\xout{of}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_004-v0008} such
\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_004-v0009} that $\phi(s) \neq 0$ for every s \ ($\phi$ being determined by $\hand{\xi}$ \ modulo
multiplication by an invertible section of $\underline{O}_S$); since $\underline{E}$ = $p_{\hand{*}}(\underline{O}_P(1))$
(p: P$\hand{\to}$S being the projection), \ $\phi$ \ can be regarded as a section
of \ $\underline{O}_P(1)$, \var{\over{therefore the}{whose}}{whose}{writing over}{ega25_25_004-v0010} divisor is precisely $H_{\hand{\xi}}$. \ Of course, when we
\var{\inserted[typed]{\uline{regard}}}{\uline{regard}}{insertion}{ega25_25_004-v0011}\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_004-v0012} $\underline{L}^{-1}$ \ as an invertible sub\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_004-v0013}module of \ $\underline{E}$ that is locally
a direct factor in $\underline{E}$, the correspondence between $\hand{\xi}$ \ (i.e. $\underline{L}$ \hand{\unsure{$\xi$,}}
\tnote{25-004-1}{The abbreviations loc and resp. are retained for locally and respectively. The doubtful n between Nous and appelerons is represented by a doubt mark on call. In (xx) the source has D, while the following sentence has L; both letters are retained. The handwritten c-to-t correction in donc/dont changes the English phrase from therefore the to whose.}

\mspage{\hand{4}}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_005}{%

\mspage{\hand{2}}

or again $\underline{L}^{-1}\subset\underline{E}$) and $\unsure{\phi|\emptyset}$ is obtained by taking \var{\inserted{for $\unsure{\phi|\emptyset}$}}{for $\unsure{\phi|\emptyset}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_005-v0001} a section of $\underline{L}^{-1}$ that
vanishes at no point \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_005-v0002}, i.e. a trivialization of $\underline{L}^{-1}$
(which in any case exists locally). Observe also that $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ is
precisely $P(\underline{E}/\underline{L}^{-1})$ (canonical isomorphism), giving a third
way to describe $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ (NB $P(\underline{E}/\underline{L}^{-1})$ is indeed canonically embedded in
$P = P(\underline{E})$), which has the additional advantage of showing that $H$ is a projective
bundle over $S$, and a fortiori is smooth over $S$ (though it would have been necessary to say \var{\xout{\unsure{th}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_005-v0003}
\var{\margin{$\|$}}{\placedmargin{$\|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_005-v0004} in par. 17 that a projective bundle is smooth ...). It would no doubt
\var{\margin{$\|$}}{\placedmargin{$\|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_005-v0005} be preferable to start with this one.
\uline{Remarks}. The formation of $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ in terms of $\hand{\xi}$ is compatible with
base change, as is immediately seen., in other words,
we obtain a homomorphism of functors $(\mathrm{Sch})^{\circ}_{/S}\hand{\to}(\mathrm{Ens})$
\[ \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P} \hand{\longrightarrow} \underline{\mathrm{Div}}\,(P/S) \qquad , \]
where the second member denotes the functor of ``relative divisors'' of $P/S$,
whose value at $S'$ (an arbitrary $S$-prescheme) is the set of
closed subschemes of $P_{S'}$ that are transverse complete intersections
of codimension 1 relative to $S'$ (cf par.19). It is easy to show
that this\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_005-v0006} homomorphism of functors is a monomorphism, in other
words that $\hand{\xi}$ is determined by $H_{\hand{\xi}}$. (This last fact justifies the terminology
``scheme of hyperplanes'' used \var{\xout{\unsure{a}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_005-v0007}above). We shall see \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_005-v0008} \var{\margin{)}}{\placedmargin{)}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_005-v0009}
\var{\xout{\unsure{Chap.V}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_005-v0010} that the functor $\underline{\mathrm{Div}}$ $(P/S)$ is representable by \var{\xout{\unsure{a}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_005-v0011}\var{\inserted{the pre}}{the pre}{insertion}{ega25_25_005-v0012}scheme that is the sum
of the $P(\mathrm{Symm}^{k}(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{E}))$, so that $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is identified with an open
and closed subprescheme of $\underline{\mathrm{Div}}$ $(P/S)$ ... (NB To tell the truth, the determination of the relative
\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_005-v0015} divisors \var{\inserted{of $P/S$}}{of $P/S$}{insertion}{ega25_25_005-v0016} could already be carried out with the means at hand, by
\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_005-v0017} using the results of Chap III, and could be added \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_005-v0018} as
\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_005-v0019} an example in par.19 ...).
\tnote{25-005-1}{The formula retains the French functor abbreviation Ens. The two French accent corrections in À vrai dire and détermination leave the English expressions unchanged; their variant identifiers are documented in the translation omission record.}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_006}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 6 (PDF page 7).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/6}
\mspage{\hand{3}}

Let us now make the base change $S'=\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}\hand{\longrightarrow} S$, and consider
the diagonal section (or\hand{``}generic section\hand{''}) of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}_{S'} = P\,(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}_{S'})$ over $S'$:
we obtain a closed subscheme $\underline{H}$ of $P_{S'} = P\times_S\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, sometimes called the
\uline{incidence scheme} between $P$ and $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, defined by the Ideal that is the image of
the canonical homomorphism
\[ \underline{O}_P(-1)\otimes_S\underline{O}_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}(-1)\hand{\longrightarrow} \underline{O}_{P\times_S\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}} \qquad ; \]
by what we have seen, it is a projective bundle over $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, and by
symmetry it is also a projective bundle over $P$. Of course, we
recover the \var{\deleted{\var{\xout{\unsure{definitions of the}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_006-v0002}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_006-v0001}\var{\inserted{``special'' hyperplanes}}{``special'' hyperplanes}{insertion}{ega25_25_006-v0003} $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ (for $\hand{\xi}$ a section of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ over $S$)\hand{,} from
the "universal hyperplane" $\underline{H}$\hand{,} by taking its inverse image under the base
change $S\hand{\xrightarrow{\xi}}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$. The same remark applies to every point
$\hand{\xi}$ of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ with values in an arbitrary $S$-prescheme $S'$, which (regarded as
a section of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}_{S'}$ over $S'$) defines an $H_{\hand{\xi}}\subset P_{S'}$: the latter is
precisely the inverse image of $\underline{H}$ under the base change $S'\hand{\xrightarrow{\xi}}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$.

We henceforth assume given a prescheme $X$ of finite type over $S$,
and an $S$-morphism
\[ f\colon X\hand{\longrightarrow} P \quad . \]
\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_006-v0005}One of the principal objects of this paragraph is to study, for every
\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_006-v0006}hyperplane $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ of $P$, \var{\inserted{$(\xi\in\overset{\vee}{P}(S))$}}{$(\xi\in\overset{\vee}{P}(S))$}{insertion}{ega25_25_006-v0007} its inverse image
\[ Y_{\hand{\xi}} = f^{-1}(H_{\hand{\xi}}) = X\times_P H_{\hand{\xi}} \quad , \]
and in particular to relate the properties of $X$ and the $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$. As
usual, we shall also have to consider $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}(S')$, with $S'$ \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_006-v0008}
an arbitrary $S$-prescheme; in this case $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ is a hyperplane of $P_{S'}$ \hand{,}
and we shall again put
\[ Y_{\hand{\xi}} = f_{S'}{}^{-1}(H_{\hand{\xi}}) = X_{S'}\times_{P_{S'}}H_{\hand{\xi}} = X\times_P H_{\hand{\xi}} \quad , \]
where the subscript $S'$ denotes, as \unsure{usual}, \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_006-v0009} the effect of the base change
$S'\hand{\longrightarrow} S$, and where in the last term $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ is regarded as a $P$-prescheme.
\tnote{25-006-1}{The source calls the diagonal section section générique, rendered generic section. The French correction from la to le before changement does not change the English article. The doubtful overtyping in habitude/hhabxitude is represented by the mark on usual.}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_007}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 7 (PDF page 8).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/7}
\mspage{\hand{4}}

by the composite morphism $H_{\hand{\xi}} \hand{\longrightarrow} P_{S'} \hand{\longrightarrow} P$. It is then convenient
\var{\xout{\illegible{4}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_007-v0001} also to consider the case where \hand{$\xi$} is
``universal'', i.e. where $S' = \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, and \hand{$\xi$} is the diagonal section, so $H \ = \underline{H}$;
in this case we shall write (unless Dieudonné suggests better notation)
$\underline{Y} = Y_{\hand{\xi}}$. In the general case of a \hand{$\xi$}: $S' \hand{\to} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, we shall therefore also have
\[ Y_{\hand{\xi}} = \underline{Y} \times_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}} S' \ . \]
Finally, when F is a Module on X, we shall \unsure{denote} by $G_{\hand{\xi}}$ its inverse image
on $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$, and by $\underline{G}$ its inverse image on $\unsure{\underline{M}|\underline{H}}$, so that we shall also have
\[ G_{\hand{\xi}} = \underline{G} \otimes_{\underline{O}_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}} \underline{O}_{S'} \ . \]
Let us summarize in a small diagram the essent\unsure{i}al features of the constructions \var{\inserted{and notation}}{and notation}{insertion}{ega25_25_007-v0002} under

consideration:
\diagram{
\begin{tikzcd}[arrows={handwritten},ampersand replacement=\&,nodes in empty cells]
F \& \& \underline{G} \& \& G_{\hand{\xi}} \\
X \arrow[d] \& X\times_S\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P} \arrow[l] \arrow[d] \& \underline{Y} \arrow[l] \arrow[d] \& \& Y_{\hand{\xi}} \arrow[ll] \arrow[d] \\
P \arrow[d] \& P\times_S\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P} \arrow[l] \arrow[d] \& \underline{H} \arrow[l] \arrow[dl] \& \& H_{\hand{\xi}} \arrow[ll] \arrow[dl] \\
S \& \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P} \arrow[l] \& \& S' \arrow[ll] \&
\end{tikzcd}
}
\var{\margin{\illegible{1}}}{\placedmargin{\illegible{1}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_007-v0003}

(The squares and diamonds in this diagram are car\var{\over{\illegible{1}}{te}}{te}{writing over}{ega25_25_007-v0004}sian).

In the next number, we systematically study the
\unsure{following} case: S' is the spectrum of a field \var{\inserted{K}}{K}{insertion}{ega25_25_007-v0005}, and its image in P is \uline{generic}
in the corresponding fiber $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}_s$. By making the base change
Spec k(s) $\hand{\longrightarrow}$ S, we are reduced for this\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_007-v0006} to the case where S is the spectrum
of a field k, which we shall assume in the next no. Besides, most
of the properties examined for X and $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ are of a ``\unsure{geometric} nature'',
and hence invariant under change of the base field; this then allows us
equally well to reduce to the case where K is algebraically closed, or to the
case where K = k(\hand{$\eta$}), \hand{$\eta$} being the generic point of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, and \hand{$\xi$}: Spec(K)$\hand{\to}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$
being, of course, the canonical morphism. We likewise observe that for
questions of a geometric nature concerning X, $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$, we may (by making
a base change on k) restrict ourselves to the case k alg closed.
\tnote{25-007-1}{The source has the alternatives déssignerons/désignerons and géométriqque/géométrique, and a doubtful u in suivant; these typographical uncertainties are represented by marks on the corresponding English words. Several line endings are clipped, including inverse and génériqu. The abbreviation alg is retained for algebraically. The handwritten té in cartésiens is rendered by te in cartesian.}

\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_008-v0001}A terminological omission: when f is an immersion, Y\hand{$_\xi$} is usually
called a \uline{hyperplane section} of X (relative to the projective immersion
f and the hyperplane H); there is no reason not to extend this
terminology to arbitrary f.

20.2. \uline{Study of the generic hyperplane section}: \hand{\uline{local properties}}.

Recall that now S=Spec(k), k a field. When \hand{$\eta$} is a point
of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, \var{\xout{xxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_008-v0003} and \hand{$\xi$}: Spec(\hand{$\eta$})$\hand{\to}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is the canonical morphism, we shall also write
H\hand{$_\eta$}, Y\hand{$_\eta$}, G\hand{$_\eta$} instead of H\hand{$_\xi$}, Y\hand{$_\xi$}, G\hand{$_\xi$}. In this no., \hand{$\eta$}
will always denote the generic point of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$.
\uline{Proposition} 2.1. Assume X irreducible\var{\inserted{\var{\deleted{\illegible{1}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_008-v0005}}}{\var{\deleted{\illegible{1}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_008-v0005}}{insertion}{ega25_25_008-v0004}. Then Y\hand{$_\eta$} is irr. \var{\inserted{(\var{\deleted{\illegible{1}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_008-v0007})}}{(\var{\deleted{\illegible{1}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_008-v0007})}{insertion}{ega25_25_008-v0006} or empty.,
and in the first case it dominates X. \var{\inserted{In any case $\overline{Y}$ is irreducible.}}{In any case $\overline{Y}$ is irreducible.}{insertion}{ega25_25_008-v0008}
Indeed, since \uline{H}$\hand{\longrightarrow}$ P is a projective bundle, so is
\uline{Y}$\hand{\longrightarrow}$ X, which implies that \uline{Y} is irreducible, since X is. Thus the generic
fiber Y of \uline{Y} over $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is irreducible or empty, and in the first case
its generic point is the generic point of \uline{Y}, hence lies over that of
X, QED.
\uline{Proposition 2}.2. Let Z be a subset of P. Then its inverse image Z\hand{$_\eta$} in
H\hand{$_\eta$} is empty if and only if every point of Z is closed. In particular,
for constructible Z, Z\hand{$_\eta$} = $\emptyset$ iff Z is finite.

We may assume Z reduced to a single point z, and we are reduced to
proving that the \unsure{image} of H in P consists \var{\inserted[typed]{exactly}}{exactly}{insertion}{ega25_25_008-v0009} of the nonclosed points of P.
Denoting by X the closure of z, and using 2.1., we are reduced to
proving that \var{\over{X}{Y}}{Y}{writing over}{ega25_25_008-v0010}\hand{$_\eta$} = $\emptyset$ iff X is \var{\xout{\illegible{3}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_008-v0011} \var{\inserted[typed]{finite}}{finite}{insertion}{ega25_25_008-v0012} (X being a closed subscheme
\var{\xout{xxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_008-v0013} of P). Replacing X by X$_{\text{k(\hand{$\eta$})}}$\hand{$\hookrightarrow$}P$_{\text{k(\hand{$\eta$})}}$, \var{\xout{xxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_008-v0014} \var{\inserted[typed]{the ``it is necessary''}}{the ``it is necessary''}{insertion}{ega25_25_008-v0015} follows from the
\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_008-v0016}following fact, for which we must have a reference, and which deserves to be
\unsure{restated} here as a lemma: for Y an \uline{arbitrary} hyperplane section of
X with Y\hand{$_\eta$}=$\emptyset$, X is finite (indeed, X $\subset$ P - H is affine, and
projective ...). The "it is sufficient" is immediate, for example by observing that
\uline{Y} is a projective bundle of relative dimension n-1 over X (\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_008-v0017}n being the
relative dim of P \var{\xout{xxxxS)}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_008-v0018} and $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ over S), so, since X is finite over k, \uline{Y} has
\tnote{25-008-1}{The source abbreviations irr., sss, and cqfd are rendered irr., iff, and QED. The quoted il faut and il suffit identify the necessity and sufficiency directions. The doubtful readings imalge/image and réexmplicité/réexplicité are represented by the marks on image and restated. The formulas, including the occurrence of Spec with eta and the later subscript on Y in the proposed lemma, are retained as transcribed.}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_009}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 9 (PDF page 10).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/9}
\mspage{\hand{6}}

absolute dimension n-1\var{\inserted{$< n = \dim \check{P}$}}{$< n = \dim \check{P}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_009-v0001}, so the morphism $\underline{Y}\hand{\longrightarrow} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ cannot be dominant,
and hence its generic fiber $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ is empty.

\uline{Corollary} 2.3. Let $f:X\hand{\longrightarrow} P$ be a morphism of finite type, and let Z be a
constructible subset of X. For its inverse image in $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ to be empty,
it is n and s that the image f(Z) be finite. In particular, for \var{\xout{X}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0002} $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ to be
empty, it is n and s that f(X) be finite.

\uline{Corollary} 2.4. Let Z,Z' be two closed \var{\deleted{irreducible}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_009-v0003}\var{\inserted{(with Z irreducible)}}{(with Z irreducible)}{insertion}{ega25_25_009-v0004} subsets of X,
and let $Z_{\hand{\eta}}$ and $Z'_{\hand{\eta}}$ be their inverse images in $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$. For
$Z_{\hand{\eta}}\subset Z'_{\hand{\eta}}$, it is necessary and sufficient that f(Z) be finite or that $Z\subset Z'$.
For $Z_{\hand{\eta}} = Z'_{\hand{\eta}}$, it is n and s that \var{\xout{ZxxxxZ}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0005} f(Z) and f(Z') be finite, or \var{\inserted[typed]{that $Z=Z'$}}{that $Z=Z'$}{insertion}{ega25_25_009-v0006}
\var{\xout{\unsure{Indeed, it is necessary to express}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0007} This is a \var{\xout{\unsure{easy consequence of 2.}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0008}
\var{\xout{\unsure{2.3}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0009} direct consequence of 2.3., since we see that $f(Z - Z\cap Z')$ can be finite
only when $Z\subset Z'$, or f(Z) is finite (for, without $Z\subset Z'$, $Z-Z\cap Z'$
is dense in Z, hence $f(Z-Z\cap Z')$ is dense in f(Z), and when the former
is finite and hence closed---being constructible---so is the latter.

\uline{Corollary} 2.5. To every irreducible component $X_i$ of X such that
$\dim \overline{f(X_i)} > 0$, let us associate \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0010} its inverse image $Y_{i\hand{\eta}}$ in
$Y_{\hand{\eta}}$. Then $Y_{i\hand{\eta}}$ is an irreducible component of $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$, and in this way we obtain
a one-to-one correspondence between the set of irr. comp.
$X_i$ of X such that $\dim \overline{f(X_i)}> 0$, and the set of irr comp of $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$.

Indeed, it follows from 2.3. that \var{\xout{X}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0012} $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ is the union of the $Y_{i\hand{\eta}}$ under consideration
and that the latter are nonempty closed subsets of Y; they are,
moreover, irreducible by 2.1. Finally, no one of them is contained
in another, by 2.4., whence the conclusion.

Observe that, for \var{\xout{\unsure{$d_i$}xxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0013} $\dim X_i$\var{\inserted[typed]{$= d_i$}}{$= d_i$}{insertion}{ega25_25_009-v0014}, \var{\xout{xxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_009-v0015} we have $\dim Y_i = d_i-1$, \var{\deleted{as
follows from the proposition}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_009-v0016} more general\hand{ly}:
\uline{Proposition} 2.6. Assume that \var{\inserted{for every irred. comp. $X_i$ of X we have}}{for every irred. comp. $X_i$ of X we have}{insertion}{ega25_25_009-v0017} $\dim \overline{f(X_{\hand{i}})} > 0$ i.e. $Y_{\hand{i\eta}} \neq \emptyset$\var{\inserted{\uline{or} that f is an immers. and $\dim \overline{f(X)}>0$}}{\uline{or} that f is an immers. and $\dim \overline{f(X)}>0$}{insertion}{ega25_25_009-v0018}. Then we have
\[ \dim Y_{\hand{\eta}} = \dim X - 1 \qquad . \]
\tnote{25-009-1}{The abbreviated French il f et s is rendered it is n and s, meaning it is necessary and sufficient. The abbreviations irr. comp. and immers. retain the source's compressed terminology for irreducible component and immersion. The handwritten ending ment in généralement is rendered by ly. The open parenthesis in the proof of 2.4 has no closing counterpart in the transcribed page.}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_010}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 10 (PDF page 11).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/10}
\mspage{\hand{7}}

We are reduced to the case where X is irreducible, by 2.5. By its very construction,
Y is defined from $X_{k(\hand{\eta})}$ as the divisor of a section
of an \unsure{invertible} module on $X_{k(\hand{\eta})}$ (namely the inverse image of $\underline{O}_P(1)$))
On the other hand, $X_{k(\hand{\eta})}$ is irreducible (since X is, and $k(\hand{\eta})$ is a
\var{\margin{|}}{\placedmargin{|}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_010-v0001} purely transcendental extension of k, which should have been pointed out at the beginning of the
no. ...), and $Y_{\hand{\eta}} \neq X_{k(\hand{\eta})}$, since the image of $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ in X (in contrast
to that of $X_{k(\hand{\eta})}$, which is faithf. flat over X) is not equal to X: it does not
contain the closed points of X, by 2.3. It follows that
dim $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ = dim $X_{k(\hand{\eta})}$ -1 \var{\inserted{(ref)}}{(ref)}{insertion}{ega25_25_010-v0002} = dim X - 1, QED.

\uline{Proposition }2.7. Let F be a quasi-coherent Module on X, giving $G_{\hand{\eta}}$ on $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$.
Let $Z_i$ be the prime cycles associated to F such that dim $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(Z_i)}}}$ $\hand{>}$ 0. Let
$Z_{i\hand{\eta}}$ be the inverse image of $Z_i$ in $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$; then the $Z_{i\hand{\eta}}$ are exactly all
the prime cycles associated to $G_{\hand{\eta}}$, and their inclusion relations
are those between the $Z_i$.

The last assertion is contained in 2.4. On the other hand, since
$\underline{Y} \hand{\rightarrow} X$ is a projective bundle, hence flat with $(S_1)$ and irr. fibers, it follows
from par.3 that the prime cycles associated to the inverse image \var{\inserted[typed]{$\underline{G}$}}{$\underline{G}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_010-v0004} of F on
$\overline{Y}$ are the inverse images \var{\xout{xxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_010-v0005} of the prime cycles associated to F,
whence, by \var{\xout{taking}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_010-v0006} \var{\inserted[typed]{restricting to}}{restricting to}{insertion}{ega25_25_010-v0007} the generic fiber \var{\inserted[typed]{$Y_{\hand{\eta}}$}}{$Y_{\hand{\eta}}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_010-v0008} of $\underline{Y}$ over $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, the fact that the
prime cycles associated to $G_{\hand{\eta}}$ are the \uline{nonempty} inverse images of the $Z_i$,
which establishes 2.7. by 2.3. To tell the truth, passage through $\underline{Y}$ is
unnecessary; one can use directly the fact that $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ \hand{$\longrightarrow$} X is flat with
$(S_1)$ fibers (and even geom. regular fibers, i.e. the morphism is regular)
and irreducible fibers (and even geometrically irreducible ones: they are
localizations of projective schemes)\hand{;} \hand{an analogous remark for the
\unsure{dim.|proof} of 2.\unsure{1}.}
\tnote{25-010-1}{The doubtful letter in invers[i|a]ble is represented by a mark on invertible. The abbreviation fid. plat is rendered faithf. flat. In the final handwritten remark, the alternatives dim. and dém. have been rendered dim. and proof; the latter French abbreviation is for démonstration. The source retains both an overlined Y and an underlined Y in the proof.}

\mspage{\hand{8}}

\uline{Proposition }2.8. Let F be coherent on X, and \unsure{let} $\var{\over{x}{y}}{y}{writing over}{ega25_25_011-v0001}\in Y$, x its image
in X. \var{\xout{\unsure{Pxxxxthatxx\&xxxxxxx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_011-v0002} Let P\var{\inserted[typed]{(M)}}{(M)}{insertion}{ega25_25_011-v0003} be one of the following properties for
a module of finite type \var{\inserted[typed]{M}}{M}{insertion}{ega25_25_011-v0004} over a noetherian local ring A:

(i) coprof $M\leqslant n$ (ref).

(ii) M satisfies$(S_k)$ (ref).

(iii) M is \unsure{Cohen-Macaulay|CMahn-Macaulay}.

(iv) M is reduced(\hand{\unsure{ref|vif}}).

(v) M is integral(\hand{\unsure{ref|vif}}).

Then, for $\unsure{E|F}_{\hand{\eta},y}$ to satisfy property P, it is n and s that $F_x$
satisfy it.

This follows immediately from the results of par.6, bearing in mind that
\var{\xout{X}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_011-v0005} $Y_{\hand{\eta}}\hand{\longrightarrow} X$ is a regular morphism, \unsure{hence} $\underline{O}_{X,\unsure{x}}\mathrel{\hand{-}} \underline{O}_{\unsure{Y}_{\hand{\eta}},y}$ is
regular. By 2.3. we therefore obtain:

\uline{Corollary} 2.\unsure{9}. With the notation of 2.8., let \var{\deleted{$\unsure{E_P(F)}$}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_011-v0006}\var{\inserted{Z}}{Z}{insertion}{ega25_25_011-v0007} be the set
of $x\in X$ such that $P(F_x)$ is not satisfied. \unsure{Then}, for
$G_{\hand{\eta}}$ to satisfy P at all points, it is n and s that $f(\unsure{Z})$ be a
finite subset of P, or again that \var{\deleted{f(Z)}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_011-v0008} dim $\overline{f(Z)}$ = 0.

Indeed, 2.8. tells us that $\unsure{h}^{-1}(Z)$ is the P-singular set
of $G_{\hand{\eta}}$, and it is empty iff f(Z) is finite, by 2.3. (NB h denotes
\var{\margin{|}}{\placedmargin{|}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_011-v0009} the morphism $Y_{\hand{\eta}}\hand{\longrightarrow} X$; I realize at this very moment that the letter P
\var{\margin{|}}{\placedmargin{|}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_011-v0010} in 2.8. does double duty).

\var{\margin{|}}{\placedmargin{|}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_011-v0011} \uline{Corollary} 2.\unsure{10}. Condition for $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ to be reduced resp. loc integral.

\var{\deleted{Care should be taken that 2.10. \var{\inserted[typed]{and 2.11. below}}{and 2.11. below}{insertion}{ega25_25_011-v0013} says nothing about the properties
``geometrically reduced'' and ``geometrically integral'', the properties
considered in 2.8. being ``absolute''. We \unsure{shall}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_011-v0012}
\tnote{25-011-1}{The source notation coprof is retained. The alternative vif is retained as an uncertain letter sequence beside the possible abbreviation ref, for reference. The source's competing readings E and F in the displayed local notation remain unresolved. The abbreviation loc is retained for locally.}

\var{\margin{11}}{\placedmargin{11}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_011-v0014}

\uline{Corollary} 2.11. Let $y\in Y_{\hand{\eta}}$; for $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ to \var{\inserted{be regular (resp.}}{be regular (resp.}{insertion}{ega25_25_012-v0001} satisfy property
$R_k$ (ref) at y\hand{,} \var{\deleted{(}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_012-v0002}resp. be normal at y), it is n and s that X satisfy
the same property at x. \var{\xout{\unsure{Remark}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_012-v0003} Let Z be the set of points of X at
which X is not \var{\deleted{\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_012-v0005} (}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_012-v0004}\var{\inserted{regular (resp. \var{\deleted{$R_k$}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_012-v0007} $R_k$}}{regular (resp. \var{\deleted{$R_k$}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_012-v0007} $R_k$}{insertion}{ega25_25_012-v0006}resp. \var{\deleted{is not}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_012-v0008} normal); for $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ to be
\var{\inserted{regular (resp.}}{regular (resp.}{insertion}{ega25_25_012-v0009} $(R_k)$\hand{,} \var{\deleted{(}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_012-v0010}resp. normal), it is n and s that f(Z) be finite i.e. dim $\overline{f(Z)}=0$.

\var{\margin{|}}{\placedmargin{|}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_012-v0011} The same proof as for 2.8. and 2.9. The various
references ensuring that Z is closed must be given (since we must know that it is
constructible to apply 2.3.).

\var{\xout{\unsure{Remark 2.12}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_012-v0012} Observe that in 2.10. and 2.11. we say nothing
about the corresponding ``geometric'' properties, the results
considered being of an \hand{``}absolute\hand{''} nature. We now Examine
properties of a geometric nature. (One could, if desired, take the
\var{\margin{||}}{\placedmargin{||}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_012-v0013}opportunity to begin a new n$^{\text{o}}$).

\var{\inserted[typed]{Assume that $f:X\hand{\to} P$ is an \var{\deleted{immersion}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_012-v0015}\var{\inserted{\unsure{unramified}}}{\unsure{unramified}}{insertion}{ega25_25_012-v0016}.}}{Assume that $f:X\hand{\to} P$ is an \var{\deleted{immersion}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_012-v0015}\var{\inserted{\unsure{unramified}}}{\unsure{unramified}}{insertion}{ega25_25_012-v0016}.}{insertion}{ega25_25_012-v0014}
\uline{Theorem} 2.12. Let $y\in Y_{\hand{\eta}}$, x its image in X. For X to be
smooth over k at x, it is n and s that $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ be smooth over $k(\hand{\eta})$ at y.

We may assume k algebraically closed. When $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ is smooth over $k(\hand{\eta})$
at y, it is regular, so, since $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ is flat over X, X is regular
at x (ref), and hence smooth over k at x since k is alg closed and therefore
perfect (ref). For the converse, we may (replacing X by
an open neighborhood of x) assume X smooth, and (by the Jacobian criterion
for smoothness) defined in an open subset U of P by p equations
as $X=V(f_1,..,f_p)$, where the differentials $df_1,..,df_p$ are
everywhere linearly independent. Introducing affine coordinates
$S_1,..,S_n$ in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ and affine coordinates $T_1,..,T_n$ in a neighborhood
of x (by choosing a hyperplane $H_\infty$ not containing x), $Y\hand{\hookrightarrow} U_{k(\hand{\eta})}$ is
then equal to $V(f_1,..,f_p,\hand{\sum_i}S_iT_i-1\ )$, and it suffices to verify that
the differentials (relative to $k(\hand{\eta})$) of $f_1,..,f_p$, $\hand{(\sum}S_iT_i\hand{)}-1$
\tnote{25-012-1}{The inserted hypothesis retains une after immersion is struck and replaced by the doubtful unramifié/non ramifié. Both candidates mean unramified; the English keeps the remaining incomplete construction an unramified. The source's capital E in Examinons is reflected in Examine. Here and elsewhere ref is a request for a reference, not a supplied citation.}

are linearly independent. These differentials are precisely

\[ df_1, \ldots, df_p , \hand{\sum_i} S_i dT_i \ . \]
Since the $df_i$ are linearly independent at every point of U, and
the $dT_i$ form a basis \var{\inserted[typed]{of $\underline{\hand{\Omega}}^1_{U/S}$}}{of $\underline{\hand{\Omega}}^1_{U/S}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_013-v0001} at every point of U, \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_013-v0002} and a fortiori

\uline{Corollary} 2.13. Let Z be the set of points of X at which X
is not smooth over k. For Y to be smooth over $k(\hand{\eta})$, it is n and s
that Z be finite. In particular, if X is smooth, so is
Y.

Follows from 2.12. and 2.3. More generally, we obtain:

\var{\inserted{Then $P(\underline{O}_{X,x}, k) \Longleftrightarrow P(\underline{O}_{Y_\eta,y}, k(\eta))$}}{Then $P(\underline{O}_{X,x}, k) \Longleftrightarrow P(\underline{O}_{Y_\eta,y}, k(\eta))$}{insertion}{ega25_25_013-v0015}

this, let Z be the set of points at which X is not smooth over
k; its inverse image \var{\inserted[typed]{$Z_{\hand{\eta}}$ in $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$}}{$Z_{\hand{\eta}}$ in $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_014-v0001} is therefore (by 2.12.) the set of
points of $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ at which $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ is not smooth over k($\hand{\eta}$). Now the codimension
of Z in X at x equals that of $Z_{\hand{\eta}}$ in $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ at y by
flatness (ref par.6). Thus one is \hand{$>$}k iff the other is,
which completes the proof.
\uline{Corollary} 2.15. Let Z be the set of points of X at which
X is not smooth over k (resp. is not geom $R_k$ over k, resp. is
\var{\margin{\unsure{//}}}{\placedmargin{\unsure{//}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_014-v0002} not separable over \unsure{R|k}, resp. is not \var{\inserted[typed]{geom}}{geom}{insertion}{ega25_25_014-v0003} normal over k). For $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ to be
smooth (resp. geom $R_k$, resp separable, resp geom normal) over k($\hand{\eta}$ ), it
is n and s that Z be finite.

\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_014-v0004} From the standpoint of exposition, 2.14. and 2.15. contain 2.1\unsure{2|3}. and
2.13. (which we could therefore dispense with stating separately); on the
other hand, the corollary is more important in practice than the theorem,
which could be given as a proposition or preliminary lemma, so as to
glorify the corollary instead.

We can vary \var{\xout{\unsure{xxxxbyxi}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_014-v0005} the case of property (iii):
\uline{Corollary} 2.16. (Still assuming f an immersion \var{\inserted[typed]{and F moreover coherent}}{and F moreover coherent}{insertion}{ega25_25_014-v0006}), under the
\var{\margin{\unsure{//}}}{\placedmargin{\unsure{//}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_014-v0007} conditions of 2.7., for $Z_i$ to be nonembedded, it is n and s that $Z_i$ be so.
When this is the case, the radicial mu\hand{l}tiplicity of F at $Z_i$ \var{\xout{(ref)}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_014-v0008}\var{\deleted{rel}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_014-v0009} \var{\inserted{rel.}}{rel.}{insertion}{ega25_25_014-v0010}
to k (ref) equals that of $G_{\hand{\eta}}$ at $Z_i$ rel to k($\hand{\eta}$).

The first assertion is contained in the last assertion of
2.7. For the second, since $Y_{\hand{\eta}} \hand{\longrightarrow} X$ is flat and hence the functor F \hand{$\rightsquigarrow$} $G_{\hand{\eta}}$
exact, we are reduced, by restriction to a neighborhood of the gen pt of $Z_i$
and dévissage, to the case F = $\underline{O}_{Z_i}$ \var{\inserted[typed]{and we may assume $Z_i = X$.}}{and we may assume $Z_i = X$.}{insertion}{ega25_25_014-v0011} . Moreover, we can begin by
reducing to the case k alg closed, \var{\inserted[typed]{then X is separable over k.}}{then X is separable over k.}{insertion}{ega25_25_014-v0012} Then \var{\xout{\unsure{$\underline{O}$xxxxx$\underline{O}_{Z_i}$xxxxx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_014-v0013} the assertion is
contained in 2.15. (iii). We conclude as usual:

\uline{Corollary} 2.17. Let Z be the set of points of X at which F
is not separable over k (ref). Then $G_{\hand{\eta}}$ is separable over $k(\hand{\eta})$ iff
Z is finite, In particular, if F is separable over k, $G_{\hand{\eta}}$ is separable
over $k(\hand{\eta})$.

\var{\margin{\unsure{bb for|to put} in statement 2.12.}}{\placedmargin{\unsure{bb for|to put} in statement 2.12.}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_015-v0001}\uline{Remarks} 2.18. the hypothesis that f is an immersion, as we see, for example, by
taking X to be a purely inseparable covering of P. \var{\xout{Cxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_015-v0003} However
\var{\xout{thexfollowingxresultxx(whichxshouldxhavexcomexearlierxasxaxconclusion}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_015-v0004}
\var{\xout{tox2x11xx)xxxxxIfxxX}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_015-v0005} \var{\xout{\unsure{Propxx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_015-v0006} if k is \uline{of characteristic zero}, 2.12. to
2.1\hand{7}. hold without assuming f an immersion. Indeed, it suffices
to verify this for 2.12., and this then follows from 2.11. and the fact
that \var{\xout{xxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_015-v0007} for an algebraic prescheme in char 0, smooth = regular.

\var{\deleted{The properties considered up to now were of an
essentially local nature. We shall end this no. by studying
global properties}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_015-v0008}

\var{\over{\unsure{3}}{20}}{20}{writing over}{ega25_25_015-v0009}.3. \uline{Generic hyperplane section}: \uline{geometric irreducibility and connectedness}
\uline{Theorem} 3.1. (Bertini-Zariski). Assume dim $f(X) \hand{\geq} 2$; and that X
is \var{\xout{geometricallyxintegralx(resp.x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_015-v0010} geometrically irreducible\var{\deleted{)}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_015-v0011}. Then
the generic hyperplane section $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ has the same property.

\var{\deleted{\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_015-v0013}(Taking account of X separable \hspace{3em} Y separable, the two statements
of 3.1. are in fact equivalent: \var{\xout{Wexcanxmoreoverxassumex}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_015-v0014} the

in terms of the extension K of k and the elements $t_1,\ldots,t_n$ of K that are the images
of a suitable system of affine coordinates of}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_015-v0012}

it) and $S_1,...S_n$ in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, we see that the field\var{\xout{\unsure{ex}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_016-v0008}\var{\inserted[typed]{of}}{ of}{insertion}{ega25_25_016-v0009} functions $L$
of $Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ \var{\xout{xxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_016-v0010} identifies with the fraction field of the integral ring
$K[S_1,..,S_n]/(\hand{\sum} t_iS_i - 1)$, where the $t_i \hand{\in} K$ are the images of the $T_i$ by
means of $f:X\hand{\to} P$. Since $\dim \hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(X)}}} \hand{>} 0$, the $t_i$ are not all
algebraic over $k$, and a fortiori they are not all zero; suppose,
e.g., $t_n \hand{\neq} 0$. We then immediately find $L = K(S_1,..,S_{n-1})$,
(a purely transcendental extension), $S_n\hand{\in} L$ being given by the equation
\[ \hand{\sum} t_iS_i - 1 = 0 \]
in terms of the $S_i$ $(1\hand{\leq} i\hand{\leq} n-1)$ and the $t_i$ $(1\hand{\leq} i\hand{\leq} n)$. On the other hand,
$k' = k(\hand{\eta})$ identifies with $k(S_1,..,S_n)$, and the canonical inclusion

\uline{Lemma} \var{\over{\unsure{2}}{3}}{3}{writing over}{ega25_25_017-v0003}.\var{\over{\unsure{8|2}}{1}}{1}{writing over}{ega25_25_017-v0004}.1. \var{\inserted{(Zariski)}}{(Zariski)}{insertion}{ega25_25_017-v0005} Let k be a field, K an extension of finite type of k, \var{\inserted[typed]{m an integer $\hand{\geqslant} 0$}}{m an integer $\hand{\geqslant} 0$}{insertion}{ega25_25_017-v0006}

\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_017-v0011} This lemma\hand{,} or lemmas that resemble it like a brother\hand{,} are
scattered all over the literature, which is why I leave you the
choice of where you will copy a proof (i.e. I do not feel
inspired to find one by my own means).

\uline{Corollary} 3.2. Assume that f is \var{\deleted{an immersion}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_017-v0012} \var{\inserted{\unsure{unramified|unramified}}}{\unsure{unramified|unramified}}{insertion}{ega25_25_017-v0013} or k of char 0,
and that dim f(X)\var{\over{$\hand{>}$}{$\geqslant$}}{$\geqslant$}{writing over}{ega25_25_017-v0014} 2. Then if X is geom integral, so is
Y\hand{$_\eta$}.

Indeed, geom integral = geom irred. + separable.

\uline{Corollary} 3.3. Assume \var{\inserted[typed]{k alg. closed, and}}{k alg. closed, and}{insertion}{ega25_25_017-v0015} that for every irreducible component $X_i$
of X we have dim $f(X_i)$\var{\over{$\hand{>}$}{$\geqslant$}}{$\geqslant$}{writing over}{ega25_25_017-v0016} 2, and assume moreover that X is \hand{$\unsure{\mathcal{G}|\mathcal{S}}$}-connected,
where \hand{$\unsure{\mathcal{G}|\mathcal{S}}$} is the set of closed subsets Z of X such that dim $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(\unsure{Z|X})}}}=0$\var{\xout{\unsure{x}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_017-v0017}
(i.e. for every such Z, X-Z is connected). Under these conditions, Y\hand{$_\eta$} is
geom connected over k(\hand{$\eta$}).

Indeed, by a lemma that should appear in par.6 with the th. of
Hartshorme, the hypothesis means that any two irred. comp.
X'\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_017-v0018} and X" of X can be joined by a chain of irred comp. $X_o$=X', ...
$X_n$=X", such that two consecutive ones have an intersection \hand{$\notin\unsure{\mathcal{G}|\mathcal{S}}$}; then
the inverse images X'\hand{$_\eta$} and X"\hand{$_\eta$} are joined by the chain of the $X_{i}$\hand{$_\eta$} \var{\xout{\unsure{x}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_017-v0019}

4.1.
We return to the general situation of no. 1, S being an arbitrary
prescheme. In addition, we assume X of finite presentation over S.
In general, observe that if P(Z,k) is a “constructible” property
\var{\xout{of preschemes over}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_018-v0005} of an algebraic prescheme Z over the field k,
then the set of $\hand{\xi} \hand{\in} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $P(Y_{\hand{\xi}},k(\hand{\xi}))$ holds is \uline{constructible},
as we see by observing that $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ is the fiber at $\hand{\xi}$ of $\underline{Y} \mathrel{\hand{-}} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$\hand{,}which
is a morphism of finite pres, and applying par.9. We have an
analogous remark for a\hand{|}property P(Z,M,k), where Z,k are as above
and M is a coherent Module on Z: if $\unsure{F|E}$ is \var{\deleted{coherent}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_018-v0006}\var{\inserted{of finite pres.}}{of finite pres.}{insertion}{ega25_25_018-v0007} on X, the set
of $\hand{\xi} \hand{\in} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $P(Y_{\hand{\xi}},G_{\hand{\xi}},k(\hand{\xi}))$ holds is constructible. On the other
hand, in the two following numbers, we have developed \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_018-v0008} in various
cases a criterion for the preceding set E to contain the generic
point of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, S being the spectrum of a field k; being constructible,
it follows that E contains a nonempty open subset: this is the passage from a
conclusion for the \var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_018-v0009}generic hyperplane section to the analogous conclusion for “sufficiently general” hyperplane sections. \var{\xout{Finally,}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_018-v0012} \hand{[}\var{\deleted{In this no. we shall
also give criteria allowing us to assert that E is
open, by applying the results of par.12}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_018-v0013}\hand{]}. Observe moreover that\var{\xout{\unsure{m|x}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_018-v0014} \var{\margin{$\downarrow$}}{\placedmargin{$\downarrow$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_018-v0015}
in the case S=Spec(k) we also have a converse: for the \uline{generic} hyperplane

closed, amounts to considering points rational over k, i.e.

hyperplane sections of X itself (without first extending the base
\var{\margin{$\hand{\rightarrow}$}}{\placedmargin{$\hand{\rightarrow}$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_019-v0001}field). This indeed follows from the constructibility of E and the fact
that $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is a Jacobson scheme.

Let us give, as examples, a few particular cases where the preceding
considerations apply:

\uline{Proposition} 4.2. Let G be a Module of finite presentation on X.
Let P be one of the following properties for a Module\var{\inserted[typed]{M}}{ M}{insertion}{ega25_25_019-v0002} on an algebraic
prescheme Z over a field K;
\var{\xout{\unsure{xxxthérixxxxxxx(i)xxxxxx(v)xxxxxxxxx2,8xxxAlxxxx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_019-v0003}
\var{\deleted{a) The set \var{\xout{of the}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_019-v0005} E of \unsure{$\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$} such that G satisfies P is
constructible}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_019-v0004}
(i) coppof (M) $\hand{\leq}$ n.

(ii) M satisfies (S$_{k}$).

(i\unsure{\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_019-v0006}}i) M is Cohen-Macaulay
(iv) M has no embedded associated prime \var{\xout{ideals}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_019-v0007}\var{\inserted[typed]{cycles}}{cycles}{insertion}{ega25_25_019-v0008}.

(v) M is separable over \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_019-v0009} K.

\var{\deleted{(vi) M is geometrically integral over K}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_019-v0010}

\var{\xout{(vii)xxMxisxgeomxtxxxintegralxoverxKx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_019-v0011} With this notation; if E denotes
the set of \hand{$\xi\in$} P such that G\hand{$_{\xi}$} satisfies P, we have:
a) E is a constructible subset of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$.

b) Assume S=Spec(k), k a field, and that F satisfies P. Assume
moreover \var{\inserted{(in case (v))}}{(in case (v))}{insertion}{ega25_25_019-v0012} that k has char 0, or that f:X$\hand{\rightarrow}$P is an \var{\deleted{immersion}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_019-v0013}\var{\inserted{\unsure{unramified|unramified}}}{\unsure{unramified|unramified}}{insertion}{ega25_25_019-v0014}. \var{\xout{\unsure{Akx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_019-v0015}
Then E contains a dense open subset of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$.

Proof. a) follows from the fact that P is a constructible
property, which was seen in par.9. As for b), it follows
from the corresponding results of the \var{\xout{two}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_019-v0016}\var{\inserted{two}}{two}{insertion}{ega25_25_019-v0017} preceding n$^{o}$s. Second thoughts\var{\inserted[typed]{on b)}}{ on b)}{insertion}{ega25_25_019-v0018}: assume
more generally that if Z is the set of points of X at which
F does not satisfy P, then f(Z) is finite i.e. dim $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(Z)}}}\;\hand{\leq}0$.

\mspage{\hand{17}}

\uline{Proposition} 4.3. Let P be one of the following properties (for an
algebraic prescheme over a field K):

(i) Z \var{\xout{satisfies}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0001} is smooth over K.

(ii) Z satisfies the geometric\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0002} property (R$_k$) over K.

(iii) Z is separable over K.

(iv) Z is geom normal over K.

\var{\margin{$\uparrow$}}{\placedmargin{$\uparrow$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_020-v0003}(\var{\over{v}{\unsure{vi|v}}}{\unsure{vi|v}}{writing over}{ega25_25_020-v0004}) Z is geom. irred. over K.

% NOTE: The following overwritten label is circled in pen.
(\var{\over{vi}{\unsure{v|vi}}}{\unsure{v|vi}}{writing over}{ega25_25_020-v0005}) Z is geom. integral over K.

Let E be the set of \hand{$\xi \in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ satisfies P. Then

a) E is a constructible subset of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$.

b) Assume \var{\xout{dx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0007} S=Spec(k), k a field\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0008}, and assume \var{\inserted[typed]{in cases (i) to (v)}}{in cases (i) to (v)}{insertion}{ega25_25_020-v0009} that k has
char zero or that f:X$\hand{\rightarrow}$P is \var{\deleted{an immersion}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_020-v0010}\var{\inserted{\unsure{unramified|unramified}}}{\unsure{unramified|unramified}}{insertion}{ega25_25_020-v0011}. Assume finally in
cases (v ) and (vi) that dim $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(X)}}}$ \var{\over{$\hand{>}$}{$\geqslant$}}{$\geqslant$}{writing over}{ega25_25_020-v0012} 2. Assume that X satisfies P,
then E contains a \var{\xout{nonempty}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0013} dense open subset of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$.

Proof identical to that of 4.2. Observe that in
cases (i) to (\unsure{\var{\deleted{i}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_020-v0014}v}), it even suffices to assume (in b) that f(Z) is
finite, where Z is the set of points of X where \var{\xout{lx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0015} P fails.

\uline{Corollary} 4.4. Consider also the property:

(\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0016}$C_m^{\var{\xout{\unsure{0|o}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0017}}$) \var{\xout{Zxisxx"geometricallyxofxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0018} " $\overline{Z}$ cannot be disconnected
by a closed subset of dimension \var{\xout{m}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0019}$\hand{\leqslant}$ m" (where $\overline{Z}$ is $Z\otimes_K\overline{K}$, $\overline{K}$
the algebraic closure of K).

Let E be the set of \hand{$\xi \in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ over k(\hand{$\xi$}) satisfies $C_m$.
Then:

a) E is constructible.

b) \var{\xout{If}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0020} \var{\inserted[typed]{Assume}}{Assume}{insertion}{ega25_25_020-v0021} S=Spec(k), k a field, \var{\inserted[typed]{and that}}{and that}{insertion}{ega25_25_020-v0022} \var{\xout{xxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_020-v0023} for every irreducible
component $X_i$ of X we ha\hand{ve} dim $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(X_i)}}} \hand{\geqslant} 2$, Then, if X over k satisfies $C_{m+1}$,
then E\hand{$_{\xi}$} contains a dense open subset U of P.
\tnote{25-020-1}{The correction of the letter x to o in Supposons does not change English Assume, so it has no English variant. The two overwritten item labels remain doubtful; the references to (v) and (vi) are not reassigned. The handwritten ending it in ait is represented by the ending ve in have. The final subscript on E is retained.}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_022}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 22 (PDF page 23).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/22}
\mspage{\unsure{\hand{19}|19}}

5. \uline{Theorems of Seidenberg type}\var{\xout{\unsure{z}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_022-v0001}

5.1.
\var{In the present no., we give conditions .... (cf the preceding
page, the text struck out by mistake).}{\restoredfrom{25/21}{https://grothendieck.umontpellier.fr/web/viewer.html?file=../25.pdf\#page=22}{%
In \illegible{1} \hand{the} present n$^o$, we give conditions \unsure{under} which
the set E considered in 4.1. is open. \var{\xout{Pxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_021-v0009} We
shall deal here with properties P of a local nature on X resp. $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$, so
that we can define the set U of $y \hand{\in} \underline{Y}$ such that (if \hand{$\xi$} denotes the image
of y in $\hand{\check{}}P$) $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ satisfies P at the point y (resp. $G_{\hand{\xi}}$ satisfies
P at y). We shall first give criteria for U to be open,
using par.12. \var{\xout{xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_021-v0015} Since in any case we have
\[ E = P - h(\underline{Y} - U) , \]
it follows that if U is open and X proper over S (and hence h proper and
a fortiori closed \var{\inserted{since $\underline{Y} \to \unsure{Y}$ proper over X and $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ separated over S}}{since $\underline{Y} \to \unsure{Y}$ proper over X and $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ separated over S}{insertion}{ega25_25_021-v0020}), then E is also open.
}}{author-directed restoration}{ega25_25_022-v0012}

5.2. As we saw in no.1, \underline{Y} is defined in $X x_S \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P} = X_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}$
as the “divisor” of a section $\phi$ of $\underline{O}_X(1)\otimes_S \underline{O}_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}(1)$, which induces for
every $\hand{\xi} \hand{\in} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ a section $\phi_{\hand{\xi}}$ of \var{\xout{$\otimes$xxxxx(1)$\otimes$xxxx xxxxxxxxxxxxxx k( )}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_022-v0002} $\underline{O}_X(1)\otimes_{k(s)}\underline{O}_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}(1)(\hand{\xi})$
(a sheaf moreover noncanonically isomorphic to $\underline{O}_X(1)\otimes_{k(s)}k(\hand{\xi}) =$
$\underline{O}_{X_{k(\hand{\xi})}}(1)$), so that $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ is precisely the \var{\xout{xxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_022-v0003} “divisor” of
this section (NB the divisor of a section $\phi$ of an invertible module \underline{L} \var{\margin{$/\!/$}}{\placedmargin{$/\!/$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_022-v0004}
is defined as the closed subprescheme defined by the Ideal that is the image of
$\phi^{-1} : \underline{L}^{-1} \hand{\longrightarrow} \underline{O}$). If \underline{F} is a Module on X, then its inverse image \underline{G}
on \underline{Y}, i.e. the inverse image of $\underline{F}\otimes_{\underline{O}_S}\underline{O}_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}} = \underline{F}_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}$ on the subprescheme \underline{Y} of
$X_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}$, is precisely the cokernel of the homomorphism
\[ \phi^{-1}\otimes \mathrm{id}_{\underline{F}_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}} : \underline{F}_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}(-1,-1) \mathrel{\hand{-}} F_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}} , \]
where the notation (-1,-1) explains itself, as Mike would say. Likewise,
$G_{\hand{\xi}}$ is the cokernel of the analogous homomorphism
\[ F_{k(\hand{\xi})}(-1\var{\over{)}{,}}{,}{writing over}{ega25_25_022-v0006}-1) \hand{\longrightarrow} F_{k(\hand{\xi})} \]
where $\hand{\xi}$ is a point of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ (and of course we have a corresponding
interpretation when $\hand{\xi}$, instead of being a point of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, denotes a point
of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ with values in an S' over S \var{\xout{\unsure{??}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_022-v0008}. ). \hand{\unsure{(}}In general, if L is an
invertible Module somewhere, $\phi$ a section defining the subprescheme
V($\phi$), then for every Module F, the inverse image of F on V($\phi$)
identifies, with the usual abuse of language, with the cokernel of $\mathrm{id}_F\otimes\phi$
$F\otimes L^{-1} \hand{\longrightarrow} F$; We say that $\phi$ is F-\uline{regular} if the preceding homomorphism
is injective. If we choose an isomorphism of F and $\underline{O}_X$, -- which
is possible locally -- so that $\phi$ identifies with a section of
$\underline{O}_X$, this terminology is compatible with that \var{\inserted{already}}{already}{insertion}{ega25_25_022-v0010} introduced elsewhere
\tnote{25-022-1}{The restored 5.1 is copied from the translated paragraph on site21, and its scan and doubt links point there. The abbreviated incipit and the instruction struck out by mistake remain in the apparatus. The source spellings sedtion, estb and conayau remain in the French and are rendered section, is and cokernel. The source names Mike without further identification. The letter F in the sentence choosing an isomorphism with the structure sheaf is retained, as are the hyphen in the first homomorphism display and all of its subscripts.}

In \illegible{1} \hand{the} present n$^o$, we give conditions \unsure{under} which
the set E considered in 4.1. is open. \var{\xout{Pxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_021-v0009} We
shall deal here with properties P of a local nature on X resp. $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$, so
that we can define the set U of $y \hand{\in} \underline{Y}$ such that (if \hand{$\xi$} denotes the image
of y in $\hand{\check{}}P$) $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ satisfies P at the point y (resp. $G_{\hand{\xi}}$ satisfies
P at y). We shall first give criteria for U to be open,
using par.12. \var{\xout{xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_021-v0015} Since in any case we have

it follows that if U is open and X proper over S (and hence h proper and
a fortiori closed \var{\inserted{since $\underline{Y} \to \unsure{Y}$ proper over X and $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ separated over S}}{since $\underline{Y} \to \unsure{Y}$ proper over X and $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ separated over S}{insertion}{ega25_25_021-v0020}), then E is also open.
}}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_023}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 23 (PDF page 24).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/23}
\mspage{\hand{20}}

\uline{Proposition} 5.3. With the preceding notation, let U be the set of
$x\hand{\in} X_{\hand{\check{}}P}$, with image $\hand{\xi}$ in $\hand{\check{}}P$, such that $\phi_{\hand{\xi}}$ is $F_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular at x. \var{\deleted{Assume
moreover}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_023-v0001} Then:
% NOTE: The encircled words of the hypothesis are placed after a), at the point indicated by the handwritten leader.
a) \var{\inserted[typed]{\hand{If} F of finite presentation and flat relative to S\hand{,}}}{\hand{If} F of finite presentation and flat relative to S\hand{,}}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0002} U is open, and $\underline{G}\hand{|}U$ is flat relative to $\hand{\check{}}P$.

b) For every $s\hand{\in}\hand{\check{}}P$, if $\hand{\eta}$ denotes the generic point of $\hand{\check{}}P_s$, U contains
$X_{k(\hand{\eta})}$.

Proof. a) Since $F_{\hand{\check{}}P}$ is \var{\inserted[typed]{of finite presentation, and}}{of finite presentation, and}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0003} flat over $\hand{\check{}}P$, the conclusion
follows from 11.3. (and even from $\underline{E}_{III}$ ... in the case S loc noeth).

b) We may assume S=Spec(k). The cycles associated with $F_{k(\hand{\eta})}$ are
\var{\inserted[typed]{\var{\xout{nonempty}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_023-v0005}}}{\var{\xout{nonempty}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_023-v0005}}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0004}
(by par.3) the inverse images of the cycles \var{\inserted[typed]{$Z_i$}}{$Z_i$}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0006} associated with F. \var{\xout{By
2.6, for example, if $Z_i$ is an integral closed subscheme subset
of X such that}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_023-v0007} If $f(Z_i)$ is finite, then by 2.3. \var{\xout{X}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_023-v0008}
$Z_{i\,k(\hand{\eta})}\hand{\cap} Y = \emptyset$, in the contrary case, by 2.6.\var{\inserted[typed]{for example}}{for example}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0009} we also have
$Z_{i\,k(\hand{\eta})}\hand{\cap} Y \hand{\neq} Z_{i\,k(\hand{\eta})}$ (by dimension considerations; 2.3., already invoked
in 2.6., is probably a better reason), which proves that $\phi_{\hand{\eta}}$
does not vanish on any of the $Z_{i\,k(\hand{\eta})}$, and therefore proves b).

\uline{Corollary} 5.4. Let V be the set of $\hand{\xi}\hand{\in}\hand{\check{}}P$ such that $\phi_{\hand{\xi}}$ is \var{\xout{E}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_023-v0010} $F_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular.
\var{\inserted{If F is of finite presentation}}{If F is of finite presentation}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0011} \var{\over{T}{t}}{t}{writing over}{ega25_25_023-v0012}hen V is constructible and contains the generic points of the
fibers of $\hand{\check{}}P$ over S; on the other hand, if \var{\inserted{moreover}}{moreover}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0013} X is proper over S\var{\inserted{and F flat over S}}{ and F flat over S}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0014}\hand{,} the set V is
open. \var{\deleted{\hand{If \unsure{$S=$ spec $(k)$|$S$ spec $(k)$} ($k$ a field), then in any case $f(\check{P}-V)$ has
codimension $\unsure{\geq|\hand{\neq}} 2$.}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_023-v0015}

\var{\inserted[typed]{\uline{Remark}}}{\uline{Remark}}{insertion}{ega25_25_023-v0016}\var{\xout{Xxxxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_023-v0017}5.5. \var{\xout{Assume}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_023-v0018} let $\hand{\xi}\hand{\in}\hand{\check{}}P$ lie over $s\hand{\in} S$, and assume
that $F_s$ has no embedded associated cycles; then we immediately see that
$\hand{\xi}\hand{\in} V$ (notation of 5.4.) also means that\var{\xout{xxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_023-v0019} no irreducible
component of supp $F_{k(\hand{\xi})}$ lies over $H_{\hand{\xi}}$ (and in this criterion we can
clearly replace $k(\hand{\xi})$ by an arbitrary extension
of $k(\hand{\xi})$). Observe that the hypothesis $(S_1)$ on $F_s$ that we have just made is

\var{\deleted{\hand{$\times$ This last fact is verified by the method of 5.3. b) and dimension
theory (we replace $\eta$ by $\xi$ such that $\underline{O}_{\check{P},\xi}$ of dim $1$))}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_023-v0020}
\tnote{25-023-1}{The phrase loc noeth abbreviates locally noetherian, and supp denotes support. The source's meulleure is rendered better; its spelling remains in the French. The insertion of the finite-presentation hypothesis changes the capital A of Alors to lower-case a, represented by Then to then. The written base point in part b), the subscript in the cited E, and the references 11.3 and par.3 are retained exactly.}

satisfied in particular when we assume $F_s$ Cohen-Macaulay (a fortiori, when
F is CM over S); moreover, in this case $G_s$ is CM (for locally it
is obtained from $F_{k(s)}$, which is so, by dividing by a $\phi . F_{k(s)}$, \var{\deleted{with}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_024-v0001} \var{\inserted{where}}{where}{insertion}{ega25_25_024-v0002} $\phi$ \var{\inserted{is}}{is}{insertion}{ega25_25_024-v0003} $F_{k(s)}$-%
regular). These same remarks could \var{\inserted[typed]{(and should)}}{(and should)}{insertion}{ega25_25_024-v0004} moreover have been made locally
above, to characterize the points of U (instead of those of V).

Now using 12.1.1. and 12.1.4., we obtain:
\uline{Theorem} 5.6. Assume F of finite presentation, flat rel. to S. Let
P be one of the properties \var{\xout{following for a module M of finite type over}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0005}
\var{\xout{a local noetherian ring\var{\inserted[typed]{\var{\xout{\unsure{algebra|xlxèxkx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0008}}}{\var{\xout{\unsure{algebra|xlxèxkx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0008}}{insertion}{ega25_25_024-v0007}\var{\inserted[typed]{\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0010}}}{\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0010}}{insertion}{ega25_25_024-v0009} A over a field K}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0006} (i) to (viii) of 12.1.1.,
or \var{\xout{\unsure{(i)|(ii)} \unsure{of} coprof M \illegible{1} (ref)}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0011} (in the case where we assume $F=\underline{O}_X$) one of the
\var{\xout{(ii) M satisfies $(S_k)$ (ref)}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0012} properties (i) to (iv)\hand{/} of 12.1.4. Let
\var{\margin{\unsure{!|1}\,\unsure{/|r}}}{\placedmargin{\unsure{!|1}\,\unsure{/|r}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_024-v0013} $U_P$ \var{\xout{(iii) M is Cohen-Macaulay}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0014} be the set of $x \hand{\in} X_{\hand{\check{}}P}$ such that, if \hand{$\xi$}
\var{\xout{xxx(iv)}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0015} denotes the image of x in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, property P is satisfied
by $G_{\hand{\xi}}$ (resp. \var{\xout{\unsure{0}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_024-v0016} $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$) at the point x, \uline{and} $\phi_{\hand{\xi}}$ is $F_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular at x.
Then $U_P$ is open, and $\underline{G}\hand{|}U_P$ is flat relative to S.

Indeed, by the very definition, we have $U_P \hand{\subset} U$ (notation of 5.3. a)),
and we apply par.12 to $U \hand{\to} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ and $F_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}\hand{|}U$.
\uline{Corollary} 5.7. Assume F of finite presentation, flat rel to S,
and supp F proper over S (e.g. X proper over S). Let $V_P$ be the set of
$\hand{\xi\in}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $G_{\hand{\xi}}$ (resp. $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$) satisfies property P, and $\phi_{\hand{\xi}}$ is
$F_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular. Under these conditions, $V_P$ is open, (and moreover it is
constructible in any case, i.e. without any hypothesis of flatness or
properness).

It seems to me that, from the standpoint of presentation, we cannot leave
5.6. as it stands, with a simple reference to conditions listed in
another fascicle; instead we shall have to make a new explicit list (i) (ii)
.... of the properties in view. Moreover, point out \var{\inserted{(in 5.1. perhaps)}}{(in 5.1. perhaps)}{insertion}{ega25_25_024-v0017} that the case P= geom.
normal (with S =Spec(k) for sure) is due to \uline{Seidenberg}.
\tnote{25-024-1}{CM abbreviates Cohen-Macaulay. The source writes Suppososons at the start of 5.7; it is rendered Assume. Its formula for division, the base S in the flatness conclusion, and the cited property ranges remain unchanged. The doubtful overtyped fragment xlxèxkx is retained as a fragment, without supplying a word.}

Here we shall combine the already known criterion for geometric
connectedness of\var{\xout{xx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_025-v0027} the generic hyperplane section (3.3.), with the
connectedness th of Zariski, to obtain a connectedness result for

We assume S=Spec(k), k a field\var{\xout{xxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_025-v0029} alg closed, \var{\inserted{X proper over S}}{X proper over S}{insertion}{ega25_25_025-v0030} \var{\xout{xx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_025-v0031}
that for every irreducible component X$_{i}$ of X, \var{\xout{we have}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_025-v0032} $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(X_{i})}}}$ has
dimension \var{\over{$\hand{>}$}{$\geqslant$}}{$\geqslant$}{writing over}{ega25_25_025-v0033} 2, \var{\xout{\illegible{1} is contained in no hyper section}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_025-v0034} \var{\deleted{Then for}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_025-v0035}
\tnote{25-025-1}{The initial cancelled ce is an incomplete fragment and is retained without supplying a whole word. Modulated and nonmodulable keep the source's modulées and non modulables; impigeable is colloquial for unintelligible, from piger, to understand. Beastly renders the familiar vache. The uncertain a-to-e correction in de is rendered of for the e-reading, with literal a retained for the unresolved alternative. Connexion is rendered connectedness here, the property of 3.3 invoked in the next paragraph. The separate cancellation of the adjective's plural s has no English counterpart.}

% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 26 (PDF page 27).
