\scanpage{25/26}
\mspage{\hand{22}}
finally that X cannot be disconnected by a closed subset Z of X such
that dim $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(Z)}}} \hand{\leq} 0$. Under these conditions, for every $\hand{\xi \in} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ is
geometrically connected.

Proof. Since \var{\xout{xesxf(X}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_026-v0001} none of the $f(X_i)$ is finite, we see
that each irreducible component $\underline{Y}_i$ of $\underline{Y}$ dominates $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$;
on the other hand, $\underline{Y} \mathrel{\hand{-}} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is proper (for $\underline{Y}$ is proper over k, being so over X,
which is so over k). On the other hand, by (3.3.), the generic fiber
$Y_{\hand{\eta}}$ of $\underline{Y} \hand{\to} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is \unsure{geometrically|geometrically} connected. Finally, $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is regular and
a fortiori geom unibranch. It now suffices to apply 15.6.3.
(\var{\xout{\unsure{xsxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_026-v0003}\var{\inserted[typed]{which is merely a variant of}}{which is merely a variant of}{insertion}{ega25_25_026-v0004} Zariski's connectedness th.) to conclude
that \uline{all} the fibers of $\underline{Y} \hand{\to} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ are geom connected, qed.

\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_026-v0005} In fact, it is not difficult, by a proof of an analogous
type, to generalize \var{\over{\illegible{1}}{6}}{6}{writing over}{ega25_25_026-v0006}.1. in the same direction as in 4.4. If you do not
want to slog through the exercise yourself, at least point this out in a
remark. Say there also that in \var{\over{\illegible{1}}{6}}{6}{writing over}{ega25_25_026-v0007}.1. we make no discrimination
against hyperplane sections that might have excess
\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_026-v0008} dimension. From the standpoint of the plan, it might be clearer to group
all the connectedness questions (including 3.3. and 4.4.) in
the same n$^{\text{o}}$.
\var{\deleted{6. \uline{Application to the construction of hyperplane sections} \var{\inserted[typed]{\uline{or multisections}}}{\uline{or multisections}}{insertion}{ega25_25_026-v0010} \uline{of specified
type}, \var{\xout{orxmultisectionsx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_026-v0011}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_026-v0009}
\tnote{25-026-1}{Slog through renders the familiar te taper. The overtyped string xesxf(X is retained as a fragment. The bare hyphen in the first displayed morphism notation remains unchanged. No generalization suggested to the addressee is supplied by the translator.}

of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ contains a point rational over k; thus with the notation of
\var{\xout{\unsure{xxx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_027-v0009}4.1., if E (defined in terms of a constructible property P) contains

On the other hand, S being arbitrary again, it is immediate that for every
s\hand{$\in$}S and every point\var{\inserted{$\xi_0$}}{ $\xi_0$}{insertion}{ega25_25_027-v0011} of the fiber $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}_s$ rational over k(s), there exists a section

again \var{\xout{xxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_027-v0012} defined\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_027-v0013} as in 4.1. in terms of a constructible property

U, at least if we shrink U sufficiently. Thus we shall have constructed a
hyperplane section\var{\inserted[typed]{$Y_{\hand{\xi}}$}}{ $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_027-v0018} of X, over an open neighborhood U of s, such that

I must, however, first point out a second thought about 4.2. and 4.3., which
should be stated in a slightly more general form (at least
in a remark): we are given an integer m, and denote by E the
set of \hand{$\xi\in$}$\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\text{P}}$ such that G$_{\hand{\xi}}$ resp. Y$_{\hand{\xi}}$ satisfies P except \var{\deleted{at most}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_029-v0001} on a
closed subset of dimension\hand{$\leq$}m (i.e. the P-singular set Z has dimension
\hand{$\leq$} m). Then a) E is a constructible subset of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\text{P}}$ and b) in the
case S=Spec(k), if F \var{\inserted[typed]{resp. X}}{resp. X}{insertion}{ega25_25_029-v0002} satisfies P except \var{\deleted{at most}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_029-v0003} on a set of
\var{\xout{xx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_029-v0004}dimension\hand{$\leq$} m+1, then E contains a nonempty open subset.

\uline{Proposition} 7.2. Assume X proper over S, and F of finite
presentation and flat over S. Let P be one of the properties (i) to (v) of 4.2.
and let m be an integer. Let s\hand{$\in$}S, and assume that \var{\xout{k(s)xxF$_s$xsatisfiesx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_029-v0005}
\var{\xout{propertyxP}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_029-v0006} the set Z$_s$ of points of X$_s$ at which F$_s$ does not satisfy
P has dimension \hand{$\leq$} m+1. Then, if k(s) is moreover infinite, there
exists a neighborhood U of s in E, and a section \hand{$\xi$} of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\text{P}}$ over U having
the following properties: for every s\hand{$\in$}U, the set Z of points
of \var{\deleted{X}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_029-v0008} Y$_{\hand{\xi,\xi(\textcolor{black}{s})}}$ at which G$_{\hand{\xi(\textcolor{black}{s})}}$ does not satisfy P has dimension
\hand{$\leq$}m, and $\phi_{\hand{\xi}(s)}$ is F$_{\hand{\xi,\xi(\textcolor{black}{s})}}$-regular\hand{,} \var{\xout{xthexModulexGxxon}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_029-v0009} Under these
conditions, the Module G$_{\hand{\xi}}$ on Y$_{\hand{\xi}}$ is flat relative to U. Finally, if k(s)
is not assumed infinite, we can still carry out the preceding
construction, after an étale extension of the type considered in 7.1.

\uline{Proposition} 7.3. Essentially the same, there is no longer an F, \unsure{we|ok}
\var{\margin{[N.B. For (v) cf 12.2.1. (x) and (xi) We can therefore add \unsure{another|another} case to 4.3. by 12.2.1. \unsure{(x)}]}}{\placedmargin{[N.B. For (v) cf 12.2.1. assume X flat rel to S, and refer to properties (i) to \var{\deleted{(xxx)}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_029-v0011}\var{\inserted[typed]{(\unsure{\var{\deleted{i}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_029-v0013}v|iv})}}{(\unsure{\var{\deleted{i}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_029-v0013}v|iv})}{insertion}{ega25_25_029-v0012} of 4.3.
instead of those of 4.2. (but taking care to make \var{\deleted{the qualifications}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_029-v0014} \var{\xout{axxxxxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_029-v0015}\var{\inserted{the qualification}}{the qualification}{insertion}{ega25_25_029-v0016}
“k\var{\inserted[typed]{(s)}}{(s)}{insertion}{ega25_25_029-v0017} of char. 0 or f:X\hand{$\longrightarrow$}P an immersion\hand{\unsure{$\xi$}}\var{\inserted{, and in case (v), for every $s\in S$, and every irred. comp. Z of X$_s$, we have $\dim f(Z)\geq 2$).}}{, and in case (v), for every $s\in S$, and every irred. comp. Z of X$_s$, we have $\dim f(Z)\geq 2$).}{insertion}{ega25_25_029-v0018}

\var{\deleted{S' over S) is constant in a neighborhood of s, hence in the present case equals 1.}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_030-v0009}

\uline{Proposition} 7.4. Let \unsure{$g$|$f$}: X \hand{$\longrightarrow$} S be a proper and flat morphism, \var{\xout{and}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_030-v0011}
let s \hand{$\in$} S, \var{\xout{\unsure{suchxthat}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_030-v0012} put n = dim X$_{s}$ and assume that the dimension of
the \unsure{set|set} of points of X$_{s}$ at which X$_{s}$ is not separable over k(s)
is \hand{$<$} n\var{\inserted{(e.g. $X_s$ separable)}}{(e.g. $X_s$ separable)}{insertion}{ega25_25_030-v0013}. Then there exists an open neighborhood U of s, and a finite
surjective étale morphism S'\hand{$\longrightarrow$}U, such that X$\mathrm{x}_{S}$S' admits a section over S'.
When k(s) is infinite, we can take S' to be a closed subprescheme
of X U.

First assume k(s) infinite. We \var{\xout{apply}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_030-v0014} proceed by induction
% NOTE: The ends of this line and several following lines are cut by the right edge of the source scan.
on n, the case n=0 being trivial: indeed, in this case there exists
an open neighborhood U of s such that X$|$U itself is étale, finite, and
surjective over U, as we see from immediate cross-references.
When n$>$0, we apply 7.3. for the property “separable”, which
allows us to \var{\xout{xxxxxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_030-v0016} replace X by a “hyperplane section” Y having
the same properties, except that n is replaced by n-1. When
k(s) is not assumed infinite, we begin by making an étale extension
of the base, it works.

\uline{Remark\hand{s}} 7.\unsure{5|\var{\over{4}{5}}{5}{writing over}{ega25_25_030-v0017}}. In particular, if X is projective and separable over S,
it locally admits étale multisections over S. But observe that
we can give examples, with X proper and smooth (but not projective) over
S, where the same conclusion fails. Of course, the hypothesis of
\var{\xout{qua}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_030-v0018} projectivity cannot be weakened \var{\inserted{in general}}{in general}{insertion}{ega25_25_030-v0019} to a quasiprojectivity hypothesis,
as we see, for example, by taking X étale and not finite over S ....

\var{\margin{Possibility of avoiding a \unsure{finite|given} set ...}}{\placedmargin{Possibility of avoiding a \unsure{finite|given} set ...}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_030-v0020}

8. \uline{Dimension of the set of exceptional hyperplanes}.

8.1.In the preceding nos, especially 2 and 3, we gave
statements asserting that the set of \hand{$\xi\in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that Y has a certain
property P is constructible and contains the generic point\var{\inserted{$\eta$}}{ $\eta$}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0001}, \var{\xout{xxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_031-v0002} or
again that the set $Z_P$ of \hand{$\xi\in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ “exceptional for P” is constructible
and nowhere dense, i.e. its closure has \var{\inserted{co}}{co}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0003}dimension \hand{$\geq$} \hand{1}\var{\deleted{r (where r=dim $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_031-v0004}) \var{\margin{\unsure{.}}}{\placedmargin{\unsure{.}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_031-v0005}
(NB we assume S=Spec(k)). In certain cases we can sharpen this statement
by giving a better \var{\over{upper}{lower}}{lower}{writing over}{ega25_25_031-v0006} bound for this \var{\inserted{co}}{co}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0007}dimension, which is important
\var{\xout{for}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_031-v0008}\var{\inserted[typed]{in}}{in}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0009} various questions. For example, when we know that this \var{\inserted{co}}{co}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0010}dimension
is \hand{$\geq$} \var{\deleted{\unsure{r}-}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_031-v0011}2, it follows that a “sufficiently general” line D of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$
does not meet $Z_P$, whence the existence (if k is infinite) of “linear
pencils” of hyperplane sections \var{\xout{(Xx)xxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_031-v0012}\hand{\unsure{Dt}} $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ (\hand{$\xi$} a geometric
point of D) which are all property P (cf no. \var{\deleted{following}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_031-v0013}\var{\inserted{$-\ -\ -\ -$}}{$-\ -\ -\ -$}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0014} for examples.
-- From the standpoint of exposition, since the results of the present no.
sharpen certain results of the preceding nos, the question arises whether
we should really do this catching-up in a separate no., or give the
sharper version as we go. Redactor decidetur.

\var{\deleted{8.2. Let Z be a \var{\deleted{constructible}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_031-v0016} subset of X, \var{\inserted{\uline{such that}}}{\uline{such that}}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0017}and consider the set\var{\inserted[typed]{Z}}{Z}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0018}
of \quad $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that its inverse image Z in Y has
\var{\inserted[typed]{strictly}}{strictly}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0019}greater dimension (i.e. its closure \var{\inserted[typed]{in Y}}{in Y}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0020}has \var{\inserted[typed]{str.}}{str.}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0021}greater dimension) than that of
the generic fiber (i.e. equals dim Z if dim $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(Z)}}}$ \quad 0, resp.
is nonempty if dim $\hand{\overline{\textcolor{black}{f(Z)}}}$ \hand{\unsure{\in|(}} 0). Then Z has codim\unsure{\var{\xout{mm}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_031-v0022}n|mms}ion \quad 2 i.e.
dimension \quad r-2. (This follows immediately from the dimension theory
of a morphism applied to $\underline{Y}$ \quad $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$). Of course, this statement can be
\var{\inserted[typed]{in terms of supp F}}{in terms of supp F}{insertion}{ega25_25_031-v0023}put in module form (and is equivalent to the statement in module form), and by applying it to the geometric
irreducible components of T, we also find that the set
Z of \quad such \var{\xout{xxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_031-v0024} that there exists an irred component of}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_031-v0015}
\tnote{25-031-1}{Rare is rendered nowhere dense. The change from majoration to minoration is represented by upper bound to lower bound. The source says sont toutes la propriété P, rendered literally are all property P. Its blank relation signs and its uncertain lettering in codimension remain unfilled. Moduler is rendered put in module form. Redactor decidetur is retained as the author's Latin-style instruction for the editor to decide.}

8.2. Let Z be the set of $\hand{\xi\in}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that dim $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ $\hand{>}$ dim X - 1,\hand{\unsure{\xi}}
and assume that for every irred component $X_i$ of X we have dim $f(X_i)$ $\hand{>}$ 0;
then Z has codimension 2 in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$. \var{\xout{(i.e. dimension \unsure{r-2|r=2})}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0001} This
\var{\inserted[typed]{2.1. and 2.2.}}{2.1. and 2.2.}{insertion}{ega25_25_032-v0002}
follows from \var{\xout{\unsure{2.5|2.3}, \unsure{2.3|2.5} (which imply that the \unsure{$X_i$|$Y_i$} are irreducible}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0003}
\var{\xout{and dominate $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0004} (which implies that every irred comp of $\underline{Y}$ dominates $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$) and from
dimension theory for the morphism $\underline{Y} \hand{\to} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$. From this
result \var{\xout{(for which it suffices to}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0005} we can give as a corollary the case where
we start from a \var{\inserted[typed]{closed}}{closed}{insertion}{ega25_25_032-v0006} \var{\xout{constructible}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0007} subset Z of X and consider the dimension of the
inverse images $Z_{\hand{\xi}}$ in the $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ ($\hand{\xi\in}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$), and we can even take
Z to be the set of $\hand{\xi\in}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that there exists an irred. component of $T_{k(\hand{\xi})}$
whose trace on $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ has too large a dimension (NB we still assume
that for every irred comp $Z_i$ of Z we have dim $f(Z_{\hand{i}})$ $\hand{>}$ 0). Finally, the
most precise statement in this direction, which follows easily from the first
statement (for X irreducible) and 2.7., is the following statement in module form:
F being coherent on X, \var{\xout{let}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0008} assume that for every associated prime cycle
T for F we have dim f(T)$\hand{>}$ 0\unsure{;|,} then the set of $\hand{\xi\in}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that
$\hand{\phi_\xi}$ is not $F_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular is (constructible and) of codimension$\hand{\geqslant}$ 2.
\var{\margin{\textbar}}{\placedmargin{\textbar}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_032-v0010} (The notation for $\hand{\phi_\xi}$ is that of no.5). We can give this as the
\var{\margin{\textbar}}{\placedmargin{\textbar}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_032-v0011} \var{\inserted[typed]{main proposition}}{main proposition}{insertion}{ega25_25_032-v0012}\var{\xout{In the same way}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0013}, and state the \var{\xout{other}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0014} preceding assertions
\var{\margin{\textbar}}{\placedmargin{\textbar}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_032-v0015} as corollaries, the proof proceeding through one of the corollaries.

\var{\deleted{Another corollary: Let T,T' be closed subsets of X, and assume
that we \var{\xout{mait}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0017} \var{\xout{\unsure{notxdimxexdimxim}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0018} $T_{\hand{\unsure{\eta}}}$ \hand{/} $T'_{\hand{\unsure{\eta|\xi}}}$ ( being the generic point of
\var{\inserted[typed]{there exists an irred comp $T_i$ of T such that}}{there exists an irred comp $T_i$ of T such that}{insertion}{ega25_25_032-v0019}
P), i.e. $f(T_i)$ is infinite and $T_i$ \hand{/} T' (cf 2.4.); then the set Z of
$\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that T T' has codimension 2. To see this, we
reduce to the case T' T (replacing T' by T T'), and where T is irreducible
(replacing it by a suitable irreducible component). Then, \var{\xout{xx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0020}
by \var{\inserted{$Z_o$}}{$Z_o$}{insertion}{ega25_25_032-v0021} what we have seen, if $\overset{\hand{\sim}}{P}$ does not belong to a certain closed
subset of codi\unsure{mension|mmnsion} 2, each of the \var{\xout{fibers}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_032-v0023} closed subsets T', T}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_032-v0016}
\tnote{25-032-1}{The inserted reference 2.1. and 2.2. remains at its recorded position before follows from. The source first says codimension 2 and later says codimension at least 2; neither is changed. The inserted French ne completes the existing negation pas, leaving English not unchanged; it has no English variant. In the cancelled corollary the slashes and missing relation signs are retained. The deleted e in chacune has no English counterpart.}

Observe that with the preceding notation, if $\hand{\xi\in}$ $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ - Z, then for every
y $\hand{\in}$ Y$_{\hand{\xi}}$ we have coprof$_y$ F$_{k(\hand{\xi})}$ = coprof$_y$ G$_{\hand{\xi}}$, and consequently: if coprof F$\hand{\leq}$n,
then for $\hand{\xi\in}\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ - Z we have coprof G$_{\hand{\xi}}$ $\hand{\leq}$ n; in particular, if F is CM, then
for $\hand{\xi\in}$ $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ - Z, G$_{\hand{\xi}}$ is CM. \var{\inserted{Finally, if F is $(S_k)$, we know that $G_\xi$ is $(S_{k-1})$ for $\xi\in\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}-Z$ (ref $E_{\uline{\mathrm{IV}}}$).}}{Finally, if F is $(S_k)$, we know that $G_\xi$ is $(S_{k-1})$ for $\xi\in\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}-Z$ (ref $E_{\uline{\mathrm{IV}}}$).}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0001}
\hand{8.3.} Observe that \var{\deleted{we have no analogous result for the property}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0002} \var{\inserted{\illegible{4} in general}}{\illegible{4} in general}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0003}
\var{\deleted{(S$_k$)}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0004}, even if F=$\underline{O}_X$, k=1, X being geom integral of dim.2 (or \var{\inserted[typed]{k=2,}}{k=2,}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0005} X being
geometrically integral and geometrically normal of dim.3). It suffices
to start from an integral projective surface \var{\xout{xxxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_033-v0006} \var{\inserted[typed]{X $\hand{\subset}$ P$^r$}}{X $\hand{\subset}$ P$^r$}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0007} over k algebraically
closed, having a point \var{\inserted[typed]{x}}{x}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0008} where X is not Cohen-Macaulay; then for every
hyperplane through x, the corresponding hyperplane section Y$_{\hand{\xi}}$ has
x as an embedded associated cycle (resp. we start from a normal variety\hand{,} \hand{(}hence
\var{\inserted[typed]{(S$_2$)\hand{)},}}{(S$_2$)\hand{)},}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0009} integral X $\hand{\subset}$ P$^r$ of dimension 3, having a point x \hand{$\in$} X at which X is not
Cohen-Macaulay, then the Y through x are not \var{\deleted{\unsure{(S}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0010} CM i.e. are
not (S$_2$) at x)\hand{.} \var{\over{i}{I}}{I}{writing over}{ega25_25_033-v0011}n these examples, the set of \hand{$\xi$} “exceptional”
for property (S$_k$) contains the hyperplane of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ defined by x\hand{$\in$}P, and has
codimension 1 (and not codimension$\hand{\geq}$2). \var{\inserted{Cf \var{\deleted{\unsure{above}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0013} 8.5. below for \var{\deleted{a}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0014} precise general result in this direction.}}{Cf \var{\deleted{\unsure{above}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0013} 8.5. below for \var{\deleted{a}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0014} precise general result in this direction.}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0012}

\var{\deleted{However, if F satisfies property (S$_k$), we can \unsure{show|show} with
the aid of the preceding results that the set of $\hand{\xi\in}$ $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $\phi_{\hand{\xi}}$ is
F$_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular\hand{,} \uline{and} G$_{\hand{\xi}}$ satisfie\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_033-v0016}s (S$_{k-1}$), has a complement of
codimension $\hand{\geq}$ 2. This follows from criterion 5.7.4. via codepth,
from the fact that the set Z$_n$ of points of X where F has codepth $>$n is
closed, and that the codimension of Z$_{n\hand{\xi}}$ in X$_{\hand{\xi}}$= Y$_{\hand{\xi}}$, although it can
make jumps (for exceptional \hand{$\xi$}) \var{\deleted{upward}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0017} \var{\inserted{downward}}{downward}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0018}, can
never jump by more than one unit, for obvious dimension
reasons.}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_033-v0015}

\var{\inserted{If F is $(S_k)$, that the set of $\xi\in\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $\phi_\xi$ is $F_{k(\xi)}$-regular and $G_\xi$ is $(S_k)$ have a complement of codim $\hand{\geq} 2$,}}{If F is $(S_k)$, that the set of $\xi\in\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $\phi_\xi$ is $F_{k(\xi)}$-regular and $G_\xi$ is $(S_k)$ have a complement of codim $\hand{\geq} 2$,}{insertion}{ega25_25_033-v0019}
\tnote{25-033-1}{The upper insertion has the written property with index k-1, while the lower insertion has index k; both remain. Coprof is retained as notation, while coprofondeur in prose is rendered codepth; the source also spells this coppofondeur. The two doubtful spellings montrer/monteer both give show. The unreadable beginning of the replacement after 8.3 remains marked.}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_034}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 34 (PDF page 35).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/34}
% NOTE: The inserted continuation after a component is typed at the top of the sheet and linked there by a handwritten placement stroke.
\var{\margin{30}}{\placedmargin{30}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_034-v0001}
Let us point out the following dimension result:

\uline{Proposition} 8.4. Let T be a closed subset of X, and assume
dodim(T,X) $\hand{\geq}$ k. Then for every \hand{$\xi\in$}$\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ we have dodim ($T_{\hand{\xi}}$,$Y_{\hand{\xi}}$) $\hand{\geq}$ k-1. Let

8.\var{\over{4}{6}}{6}{writing over}{ega25_25_037-v0001}. We now study the points y of $\underline{Y}$ that are not smooth
on $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ rel to $k(\hand{\xi})$. We restrict ourselves to the case where $f:X\hand{\to} P$ is \uline{unramified}
(in practice, it will be an immersion), and where $X\hand{\to} S$ is smooth. We do not
necessarily assume S to be the spectrum of a field. \var{\xout{We restrict ourselves}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_037-v0002}
\var{\xout{to considering the points of $X_P$ that are in the open subset U of}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_037-v0003} Since
f is unramified, the canonical homomorphism $f^*(\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S})\hand{\to}\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{X/S}$
is surjective, and its kernel is a loc free Module on X, which we
shall denote by $\hand{\check{\nu}}_{X/P}$; when f is an immersion, it is precisely the
\uline{conormal} Module $\underline{J}/\underline{J}^2$ defined by the Ideal $\underline{J}$ of X in P, and we shall
in any case call it the conormal Module:
\[ \hand{(a)} \qquad 0\hand{\to}\hand{\check{\underline{\nu}}}_{X/P}\hand{\to} f^*(\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S})\hand{\to}\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{X/S}\hand{\to} 0 \ . \]
\var{\margin{\hand{$|||$}}}{\placedmargin{\hand{$|||$}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_037-v0004}
% NOTE: Three parallel pencil sidestrokes stand beside the canonical exact-sequence paragraph.
Note that we also have a canonical exact sequence on P
(which could appear as an example in par.16, for instance)
\[ \hand{(b)} \qquad 0\hand{\to}\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S}(1)\hand{\to}\underline{E}_P\hand{\to}\underline{O}_P(1)\hand{\to} 0 \]
(i.e. $\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S}$ is can isomorphic to the kernel of the canonical homomorphism
$\underline{E}_P(-1)\hand{\to}\underline{O}_P$ deduced from $\underline{E}_P\hand{\to}\underline{O}_P(1)$), whence, on applying $f^*$:
\[ \hand{(b')} \qquad 0 \hand{-} f^*(\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S})(1) \hand{-} \underline{E}_X \hand{-} \underline{O}_X(1)\hand{\to} 0 \ \hand{\unsure{)|,}} \]
which gives an explicit description of $f^*(\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S})$ \var{\xout{\unsure{of X|of X}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_037-v0005} on X, and
thus allows us to identify $\hand{\check{\underline{\nu}}}_{X/P}(1)$ with a sub-Module that is a loc direct summand
of $\underline{E}_X$, or again its \var{\xout{conormal Module}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_037-v0006} dual $\hand{\underline{\nu}}_{X/P}(-1)$ with a quotient Module
of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}_X$. Consequently, $P(\hand{\underline{\nu}}_{X/P}\var{\deleted{\unsure{*}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0007}(-1)\ )$ = $P(\hand{\underline{\nu}}_{X/P})$ embeds
canonically in $P(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}_X) = X\times_S\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P} = X_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}$ as a projective subbundle over
X, hence as a closed subprescheme. The latter is necessarily contained
in $\underline{Y}$ (because $\hand{\underline{\Omega}}_{X/P}(1)$ is contained in the kernel of $\underline{E}_X\hand{\to}\underline{O}_X(1)$)
\var{\deleted{The underlying set of this subprescheme is precisely the set of}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0008}
\var{\deleted{\var{\xout{xxxxP}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_037-v0010} $y\in\overline{Y}$ \var{\xout{such that}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_037-v0011} \uline{that are} \var{\inserted{\var{\deleted{\unsure{zeros x singular for}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0013}}}{\var{\deleted{\unsure{zeros x singular for}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0013}}{insertion}{ega25_25_037-v0012} \var{\deleted{“singular on $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$” in the following sense:}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0014}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0009}
\var{\deleted{\var{\margin{singular zeros}}{\placedmargin{singular zeros}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_037-v0016} \var{\deleted{a point is called “nonsingular” on $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ if $\hand{\phi_\xi}$ is \unsure{regular}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0017} on $X_{k(\hand{\xi})}$}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0015}
\var{\deleted{at y and \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_037-v0019} $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ is smooth over $k(\hand{\xi})$ at y.}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_037-v0018}

over k at x. The stated characterization of the \var{\xout{points}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_038-v0007} singular zeros as
the elements of the smooth subprescheme P( $\hand{\underline{\nu}}_{X/P}$) of $X_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}}$ follows immediately
from the following statement, which deserves to appear as a preliminary proposition:
if S=Spec(k), and if H is a hyperplane of P, then Y = X$\mathrm{x}_P$H is

of course from the exact sequence (b) above, which allows us to\var{\xout{identify}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_038-v0015}
define a one-to-one correspondence between the set of vector subspaces
of the tangent space at a and the linear subvarieties of
P containing a. This correspondence moreover amounts to associating to

0 \arrow[r] \& \hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S} \otimes \underline{O}_Z(1,0) \arrow[r] \arrow[u] \& E\otimes\underline{O}_Z \arrow[r] \arrow[u] \& \underline{O}_Z(1,0) \arrow[r] \& 0 \\
 \& \hand{\check{\underline{\nu}}}_{X/P} \otimes \underline{O}_Z(1,0) \arrow[u] \& \underline{O}_Z(0,-1) \arrow[u] \arrow[ul, dashed, "\hand{\beta}"'] \arrow[ur, "\hand{\alpha}"'] \& \& \\
 \& 0 \arrow[u] \& \hand{0} \arrow[u] \& \&

% NOTE: All diagram connectors are drawn in pen. The script-letter entry stands directly above E tensor O_Z; its check is above the indexed P. The first top zero has two overlapping loops of unresolved operation. The top tensor expression includes an opening parenthesis and comma, with O/Z overprinting.

where the first column is deduced from (a) by tensoring with $\underline{O}_Z(1,0)$,
the row is deduced from (b) by tensoring with $\underline{O}_Z$, and column 2
is deduced from the \var{\inserted[typed]{transpose of the}}{transpose of the}{insertion}{ega25_25_040-v0001} analogous \var{\xout{\unsure{relative}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_040-v0002} sequence (b$\hand{^\vee}$) relative to $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ (obtained
by replacing $\underline{E}$ with $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}$) by tensoring with $\underline{O}_Z$. By the very definition
of $\underline{Y}$, Z lies over $\underline{Y}$ if and only if the composite
morphism \hand{$\alpha$} in the diagram is zero, i.e. if one can find a factorization
\hand{$\beta$}: $\underline{O}_Z(0,-1) \hand{\rightarrow} \hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S}\otimes \underline{O}_Z(1,0)$. When this is so, one can
consider its composite with $\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{P/S} \otimes \underline{O}_Z(1,0) \hand{\rightarrow} \hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{X/Y} \otimes \underline{O}_Z(1,0)$,
which can also be interpreted as a section of $\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{X/Y}\otimes \underline{O}_Z(1,1)$.
I say that this is precisely the section \var{\inserted{$\mu$}}{$\mu$}{insertion}{ega25_25_040-v0003} that we had considered above.
(The verification must be essentially mechanical.) It is
zero if and only if Z lies over V(\hand{$\mu$}) (by the very definition
of V(\hand{$\mu$})!), but this also means that \hand{$\beta$} factors through
$\hand{\check{\underline{\nu}}}_{X/P} \otimes \underline{O}_Z(1,0)$, i.e. that the sub-Module $\underline{O}_Z(0,-1)$ of $E\otimes \underline{O}_Z$ is contained
in the sub-Module $\hand{\underline{\nu}}_{X/P} \otimes \underline{O}_Z(1,0)$, which clearly also means
that Z lies over the subprescheme P( $\hand{\underline{\nu}}_{X/P}$ (1)) of P($\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}_X$), and
completes the proof of what we had announced.

Even before this learned exercise in syntax (which, moreover, cost me

\unsure{quite a bit} of sweat already), one could have observed that, \var{\deleted{Since}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_041-v0001} in
any case from the set-theoretic point of view $\underline{Y}^{sing}$ \var{\inserted[typed]{is of}}{is of}{insertion}{ega25_25_041-v0002} \var{\xout{of the fibers of}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0003}dimension
r-1 \var{\xout{over S, whereas if}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0004} if S=Spec(k), whereas $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is of dimension
r, \var{\inserted{hence}}{hence}{insertion}{ega25_25_041-v0005} the image of $\underline{Y}^{sing}$ in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ is of codimension $\hand{\geq}$1, which gives
2.12 again. (The argument is of course not essentially distinct from
that used in 2.12.) Note that most often this set
does indeed have codimension 1 (cf below). Consequently, one will in general
be unable to find “linear pencils” of hyperplane sections
that are all \var{\inserted[typed]{\var{\xout{geom}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0008}}}{\var{\xout{geom}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0008}}{insertion}{ega25_25_041-v0007} \var{\xout{nonsingular}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0009} smooth. However, we shall see that
it will often be possible to arrange \var{\xout{that}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0010} to find pencils consisting of hyperplane
sections with no supersingular point, thanks to the fact that
in the most frequent cases, the image of $\underline{Y}^{sup\ sing}$ in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ will be of
codimension 2. We shall first summarize the essential
differential aspects of the situation studied here:

\uline{Theorem} 8.\var{\over{\unsure{5|3}}{7}}{7}{writing over}{ega25_25_041-v0011}. a) The subprescheme $\underline{Y}^{sing}$ (defined in par.16) in
the present situation is precisely $P(\underline{\hand{\nu}}_{X/P})$, regarded as
a subprescheme of $\underline{Y}$ as explained above.

b) The underlying set of the prescheme $\underline{Y}^{sup\ sing}$ (cf par.16), \var{\xout{\unsure{i.e.}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0013}
\var{\xout{closed in}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0014} is precisely the set of ramification points of \unsure{the} \var{\inserted[typed]{morphism of smooth preschemes over S of relative dim r-1 and r:}}{morphism of smooth preschemes over S of relative dim r-1 and r:}{insertion}{ega25_25_041-v0015}
$\underline{Y}^{sing} = P(\underline{\hand{\nu}}_{X/P}) \hand{\longrightarrow} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, i.e. for this last morphism to be
unramified at the point \var{\over{$x$}{$y$}}{$y$}{writing over}{ega25_25_041-v0016} (ref to definition) it is n and s that \var{\over{$x$}{$y$}}{$y$}{writing over}{ega25_25_041-v0017}
be geometrically an ordinary singular point for $\hand{\phi}_{\hand{\xi}}$ ($\hand{\xi}$ being the
point of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ that is the image of \var{\over{$x$}{$y$}}{$y$}{writing over}{ega25_25_041-v0018}).

c) Suppose S=Spec(k), and that \var{\over{$x$}{$y$}}{$y$}{writing over}{ega25_25_041-v0019} \var{\inserted{$\in$}}{$\in$}{insertion}{ega25_25_041-v0020}\var{\inserted[typed]{$\underline{Y}^{sing} = P(\underline{\hand{\nu}})$}}{$\underline{Y}^{sing} = P(\underline{\hand{\nu}})$}{insertion}{ega25_25_041-v0021} is rational over k; let x
and $\hand{\xi}$ be its projections in X(k) \unsure{resp} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$(k), and consider the \var{\xout{subspace}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_041-v0022}
linear subvariety \var{\inserted{$H'$}}{$H'$}{insertion}{ega25_25_041-v0023} of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ that is the “image” of the tangent map of

$\underline{Y}^{\mathrm{sing}} = P(\underline{\hand{\nu}}_{X/P}) \hand{\longrightarrow} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$. Then H' is “orthogonal” to x, and in particular

Proof. We have already proved a), and b) is a consequence of it
by the developments in par.16. It remains to prove c), which is
again a consequence of an essentially trivial compatibility
relation. I merely copy here, in the special case under
consideration (but with arbitrary S), diagram (D') of par.16, which
takes the following form (where we write $\underline{Y}^1$ for $\underline{Y}^{\mathrm{sing}}$):

\diagram{
\begin{tikzcd}[arrows={handwritten},column sep=small,ampersand replacement=\&,nodes in empty cells]
 \& \& 0 \& \& \\
 \& \& \hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{X/S\,\underline{Y}^1} \arrow[u] \& \& \\
0 \arrow[r] \& \underline{P}^1(\underline{O}(1,1))\otimes\underline{O}_{\underline{Y}^1} \arrow[r] \arrow[ur, "\hand{\unsure{\nu}.\mu}"] \& (\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{X/S})_{\underline{Y}^1} + (\hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}/S})_{\underline{Y}^1} \arrow[r] \arrow[u] \& \hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{\underline{Y}^1/S} \arrow[r] \& 0 \\
 \& \underline{O}_{\underline{Y}^1}(-1,-1) \arrow[u] \arrow[r, "\hand{u}"] \& \hand{\underline{\Omega}}^1_{\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}/S\,\underline{Y}^1} \arrow[u] \arrow[ur, "\hand{\nu}"'] \& \& \\
\hand{\underline{\omega}}^{-2}_{X/Y}\otimes\underline{O}_{\underline{Y}^1}(-2,-2) \arrow[ur] \& \& \hand{0} \arrow[u] \& \&
\end{tikzcd}
}

The orthogonality relation c) simply expresses that the composite
$\hand{\nu}u$ in this diagram is zero; I dispense with developing
this second exercise in syntax any further. I suppose people were
used to choosing coordinates, and perhaps they saw things more
clearly that way ....

\uline{Corollary} 8.\var{\over{\illegible{1}}{8}}{8}{writing over}{ega25_25_042-v0003}. Suppose S=Spec(k), \var{\xout{\illegible{1} so that $\underline{Y}^{\mathrm{sing}}$}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_042-v0004} \var{\inserted{$f : X \to P$ an immersion}}{$f : X \to P$ an immersion}{insertion}{ega25_25_042-v0005} and X
irreducible \var{\inserted{(still smooth over k)}}{(still smooth over k)}{insertion}{ega25_25_042-v0006}. \var{\xout{\unsure{Then}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_042-v0007} Then $\underline{Y}^{\mathrm{sing}}$ is smooth and irreducible; let T be

the closure of its image in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$, endowed with the induced reduced
structure, and consider the induced morphism g: $\underline{Y}^{\text{sing}} \hand{\longrightarrow} T$ \var{\xout{of}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_043-v0001} (a dominant
morphism of \var{\xout{irreducible}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_043-v0002} integral preschemes). Then the following conditions
\var{\inserted[typed]{(i) to (ii bis)}}{(i) to (ii bis)}{insertion}{ega25_25_043-v0003} are equivalent:

(i) The morphism g is generically \var{\deleted{unramified}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_043-v0004} \var{\inserted{étale}}{étale}{insertion}{ega25_25_043-v0005} (i.e. it is \var{\deleted{unramified}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_043-v0006} \var{\inserted{étale}}{étale}{insertion}{ega25_25_043-v0007} at at least one point, or equivalently, it is \var{\inserted{étale =}}{étale =}{insertion}{ega25_25_043-v0008} unramified
at the generic point of $\underline{Y}^{\text{sing}}$).

(i bis) The field extension L/K defined by g is finite and
separable.

\var{\margin{NB. The hyp. that f is an \uline{imm.} (in place of: unramified) is used only for the equivalence with (i ter). State 8.8 accordingly\dots}}{\placedmargin{NB. The hyp. that f is an \uline{imm.} (in place of: unramified) is used only for the equivalence with (i ter). State 8.8 accordingly\dots}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_043-v0009}
(i ter) The \unsure{morphism} g is birational, i.e. the extension L/K is
the trivial extension.

(ii) $\underline{Y}^{\text{sihg}} \hand{\neq} \underset{\hand{\unsure{\cdot}}}{\underline{Y}}^{\text{sup sing}}$ \var{\inserted{(set-theoretically, say).}}{(set-theoretically, say).}{insertion}{ega25_25_043-v0010}

(ii bis) There exist an $x \hand{\in} X(\bar{k})$ and a tangent \var{\over{h}{h}}{h}{writing over}{ega25_25_043-v0011}yperplane \var{\inserted[typed]{H}}{H}{insertion}{ega25_25_043-v0012} to $X_{\bar{k}}$
at x that is not “osculating” at x (by which we mean precisely
that x is not supersingular for the section of $\underline{O}_{X_{\bar{k}}}(1)$ defining
H ...).

These conditions imply that \var{\inserted{$\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}} \neq \emptyset$ or}}{$\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}} \neq \emptyset$ or}{insertion}{ega25_25_043-v0013} dim $\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}}$ \var{\deleted{$\leq$}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_043-v0014}$= r-2$, hence that
the image of $\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}}$ in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ has codimension $\hand{\geqslant} 2$, and \var{\xout{that T
is of dimension r-1, i.e. of codimension 1 in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_043-v0015} they also
imply:

(iii) dim T = r-1, i.e. T is of codimension 1 in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$.

Proof. The equivalence of (i) and (i bis) is trivial, its equivalence
with (ii) is a trivial consequence of 8.\var{\over{\unsure{5|6}}{7}}{7}{writing over}{ega25_25_043-v0016}. b), and finally the equivalence
of (ii) and (ii bis) is practically the definition of
$\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}}$. Clearly (i ter) \hand{$\Longrightarrow$}(i), and it remains to prove that (i)\hand{$\Longrightarrow$}
(i ter). We may clearly assume k alg closed, and we are reduced to
proving (in view of hypothesis (\unsure{ii|iii})) that there is an open subset U\hand{$\neq\emptyset$}

\mspage{\hand{41}}

hg$_1$: $g^{-1}(U) \hand{\longrightarrow}$ PxT is precisely the canonical inclusion, since
this holds for its restriction to $g^{-1}(V) \hand{\rightarrow}$ V. \var{\xout{Let then \unsure{U'|U} = h$^{-1}$(}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_045-v0001}
\var{\xout{Since h takes}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_045-v0002} It follows that h \var{\xout{takes its values in}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_045-v0003} factors
through the schematic closure \var{\xout{of}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_045-v0004} $\overset{\hand{\rule{1.1em}{0.35pt}}}{Y^1}$ of Y$^1$ in PxT, hence that
the inverse image of Y$^1$ (which is an open subset of the said closure) under h
is an open subset of U, say U'. In view of h g$_1$ = inclusion, we
then see immediately that g$_1$ induces an isomorphism $g^{-1}(U) \hand{\simeq}$ U', and
\var{\xout{since}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_045-v0005} this does show that $g^{-1}(U) \hand{\longrightarrow}$ U is an open immersion.
When X is proper over k, i.e. closed in P, then g is proper and hence
surjective, hence g$_1$: $g^{-1}(U) \hand{\longrightarrow}$ U is surjective, hence an isomorphism.
Moreover, if W is an open subset of T such that $g^{-1}(W) \hand{\longrightarrow}$ W is an isomorphism,
it follows that W is smooth since Y$^1$ is smooth, hence W$\hand{\subset}$U.
This completes the proof of 8.\var{\over{\unsure{8}}{9}}{9}{writing over}{ega25_25_045-v0006}.

\var{\xout{Remark}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_045-v0007} The final assertion\hand{s} of 8.\var{\over{\unsure{7}}{8}}{8}{writing over}{ega25_25_045-v0009}.\hand{,} \var{\inserted{$\underline{Y}^{\text{sup}}\text{sing}=\emptyset$ or}}{$\underline{Y}^{\text{sup}}\text{sing}=\emptyset$ or}{insertion}{ega25_25_045-v0010} dim $\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}}$ \var{\over{=}{\unsure{$\leq$}}}{\unsure{$\leq$}}{writing over}{ega25_25_045-v0011} r-2\hand{,} and

ating to X at a point $x\hand{\in} X(k)$, \var{\xout{but}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_046-v0001} such that there is \uline{another point}
$x'\hand{\in} X(k)$ at which the same hyperplane is tangent at x. We make
obvious examples with \var{\xout{examples}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_046-v0002} P of dimension 2, X a nonsingular
curve of degree $\hand{\geqslant}$ \unsure{4|5}, in arbitrary characteristic. \var{\inserted{[NB the “double tangents” = X corresponding to the double pts of the “dual curve”!].}}{[NB the “double tangents” = X corresponding to the double pts of the “dual curve”!].}{insertion}{ega25_25_046-v0003}

\uline{Corollary} 8.\var{\over{\unsure{9|10}}{11}}{11}{writing over}{ega25_25_046-v0004}. Suppose k has characteristic zero. Then:

a) The image of $\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}}$ in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ has codimension $\hand{\geqslant} 2$.

b) Condition (iii) of 8.\var{\over{\unsure{7}}{8}}{8}{writing over}{ega25_25_046-v0005}. is equivalent to the other conditions, i.e.
the negation of the other conditions\hand{,} \var{\inserted{except $\underline{Y}^{\text{sing}}=\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}}$,}}{except $\underline{Y}^{\text{sing}}=\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}}$,}{insertion}{ega25_25_046-v0006} also means that the image of $\underline{Y}^{\text{sing}}$
in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ has codimension $\hand{\geqslant} 2$.

Clearly assertion b) implies a), in view of 8.\var{\over{\unsure{7}}{8}}{8}{writing over}{ega25_25_046-v0007}. Now
by dimension theory, (iii) means that L/K is a finite extension,
\var{\xout{and in}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_046-v0008} (we could put this in the form (iii bis) in 8.\var{\over{\unsure{7}}{8}}{8}{writing over}{ega25_25_046-v0009}.),
and in char 0 \var{\xout{this implies}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_046-v0010} L is in any case separable over K, whence
condition (i bis) of 8.\var{\over{\unsure{7}}{8}}{8}{writing over}{ega25_25_046-v0011}.

\uline{Remarks} 8.1\var{\over{1}{2}}{2}{writing over}{ega25_25_046-v0012}.. a) Geometrically, assertion a) essentially means
that for a sufficiently general linear pencil of hyperplane
sections, all members of the pencil are smooth or have \var{\xout{only points}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_046-v0014}
\var{\xout{gé}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_046-v0015} geometrically as their only points geometrically ordinary
singular points (and in fact, as we see immediately --- and it
would be appropriate to state a) accordingly in a slightly more
precise form --- have at most \uline{one} such geometrically singular point). Assertion
b) \unsure{means} \var{\inserted{essentially}}{essentially}{insertion}{ega25_25_046-v0017} that when $\underline{Y}^{\text{sing}}=\underline{Y}^{\text{sup sing}}$ (which is expressed
analytically by \unsure{the}\var{\xout{vanishing}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_046-v0018} \var{\inserted[typed]{vanishing}}{vanishing}{insertion}{ega25_25_046-v0019} of a certain section D of an invertible
Module $\hand{\underline{\omega}}^{\otimes 2}_{X\hand{/}k}\otimes\underline{O}_{\underline{Y}^1}(1,1)$ on $\underline{Y}^1$), then for a sufficiently general linear pencil
of hyperplane sections, all members of this \var{\xout{sheaf}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_046-v0020}
pencil are smooth. This second situation (whether we are, moreover,
in char.0 or not) must be regarded as quite exceptional

In classical language, it is expressed \var{\xout{\illegible{1} saying}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_047-v0001}, unless I am mistaken, by saying

to explain and justify such terminology, should
you feel inspired to make the connection with the taupe\var{\over{)}{]}}{]}{writing over}{ega25_25_047-v0002}. \var{\inserted{For example, if dim $X=1$, this implies that X is a line, for if $x \in X(k)$, then T contains the lin variety L of codim 2 of hyperplanes tangent to X at x, hence for dim. reasons we have \unsure{-|:} T = L, hence $X \subset L^{\circ}$ = polar of L, which is a line, hence X is an \uline{open subset} of a line.}}{For example, if dim $X=1$, this implies that X is a line, for if $x \in X(k)$, then T contains the lin variety L of codim 2 of hyperplanes tangent to X at x, hence for dim. reasons we have \unsure{-|:} T = L, hence $X \subset L^{\circ}$ = polar of L, which is a line, hence X is an \uline{open subset} of a line.}{insertion}{ega25_25_047-v0003}

b) When the char. is p >0, to do things properly we should give
examples (with di\unsure{m|p} P = 2, X a nonsingular curve) where the
conditions of 8.6 do not hold, i.e. $\underline{Y}^{\mathrm{sing}} = \underline{Y}^{\mathrm{sup\ sing}}$, and where
nevertheless dim T = r-1, i.e. \var{\inserted{examples}}{examples}{insertion}{ega25_25_047-v0004} where L/K is a finite inseparable
extension. I am too lazy to construct examples, but I hardly
doubt that there are some.

\var{\deleted{\uline{Corollary} 8.11.}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_047-v0005} In a), give a nudge toward the \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_047-v0006} next no.,

X is called “\uline{ruled}” for f if $\mathrm{Im}(\underline{Y}^{\mathrm{sing}} \hand{\to} \check{P})$ has codim $\geq 2$}}{\placedmargin{N.B. by a trivial modification of the projective immersion under consideration,

every irreducible component \var{\inserted{$X_i$}}{$X_i$}{insertion}{ega25_25_049-v0001} of dimension 1 \var{\deleted{$X_{\unsure{i|a}}$}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_049-v0002} of X \var{\xout{either}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_049-v0003}

b) Suppose X geom. normal, \var{\xout{In order}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_049-v0008} for the set \var{\xout{Xxd}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_049-v0009} of $\hand{\xi \in}\, \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$

\var{\xout{(I forgot da before a) and b) to state the following hypothesis}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_049-v0013}
\var{\xout{always satisfied in char 0 for every irr comp $X'_i$ of $X'_i$,}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_049-v0014}

\uline{Remark} 8.15. In 8.6, 8.13 and 8.14 we imposed on X
the hypothesis $(S_k)$ (resp. $(R_k)$, resp: separable, resp. \var{\inserted[typed]{geom}}{geom}{insertion}{ega25_25_049-v0016} normal) that
we wished to obtain as a conclusion for the hyperplane sections
except \var{\inserted{for $\xi$ in}}{for $\xi$ in}{insertion}{ega25_25_049-v0017} an exceptional set of codi\unsure{mension|mension} $\hand{\geqslant}$ 2. This does not restrict
generality (and, to tell the truth, it would have cost no more
to dispense with this preliminary hypothesis), for we see immediately
using the results of par.3.4 \var{\inserted{and 5.12.}}{and 5.12.}{insertion}{ega25_25_049-v0018} that if X does not satisfy
the hypothesis in question, hence (by par.5) if there is a \uline{closed}
point x at which this hypothesis fails, then for every $\hand{\xi}$ such
that $\phi_{\hand{\xi}}$ is $X_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular (a condition excluding only a set of codim$\hand{\geqslant}$2)
and such that $x \hand{\in} Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ (a condition describing a set of codimension exactly
1), $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ also fails to satisfy the said hypothesis at x,
\var{\over{\unsure{it}}{hence}}{hence}{writing over}{ega25_25_049-v0019} the exceptional set $Z \hand{\subset} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ has codimension 1 and not 2.
(I may have gone a little too far in the case $(R_k)$, \var{\over{or}{where}}{where}{writing over}{ega25_25_049-v0020} we still need
conditions $(S_1)$ and perhaps equidimensionality.....).

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_050}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 50 (PDF page 51).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/50}
\mspage{\hand{46}}

\var{\xout{\uline{Proposition} 8.16 We assume f:X P unramified at}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_050-v0001}

Second thoughts. In 8.13 and 8.14, it suffices \var{\inserted{to assume}}{to assume}{insertion}{ega25_25_050-v0002} that f:X\hand{$\to$} P is unramified
at the smooth points of X; for the “it suffices”, it even suffices
that it is \var{\inserted[typed]{un}}{un}{insertion}{ega25_25_050-v0003}ramified on an open subset X' of X whose complement has
codimension \hand{$\geqslant$} \unsure{R|2|k}+1.

\uline{Proposition} 8.16. Suppose f:X\hand{$\to$} P unramified on an open subset
complementary to a set of codimension \hand{$\geqslant 2$,} X geom. normal and
of depth\hand{$\geqslant$} 3 at its closed points, and finally X geom. integral and proper
over k. Then the set of \hand{$\xi \in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that \var{\xout{\hand{$\phi$} is $X_{k(\ )}$ regular}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_050-v0004}
Y\hand{$_\xi$} is geom. normal and geom integral of dimension dim X - 1 (\unsure{is|is}
constructible and) has a complement of codimension \hand{$\geqslant$} 2.

Indeed, by 8.14 b), this holds for the property
“Y\hand{$_\xi$} is geom normal of dimension dim X - 1” (the dimension
property expresses that \hand{$\phi_\xi$} is X$_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular). Now by 6.1,
\uline{\unsure{all}} the Y\hand{$_\xi$} are geom connected. And if Y\hand{$_\xi$} is geom normal, it is
geom integral iff it is geom connected, which completes the proof.

\uline{Remarks} 8.17. a) \var{\inserted[typed]{The hypothesis of 8.16 implies dim X \hand{$\geqslant$} 3.}}{The hypothesis of 8.16 implies dim X \hand{$\geqslant$} 3.}{insertion}{ega25_25_050-v0006} It is possible that, whenever X is geom \var{\xout{integral}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_050-v0007}irred
and dim f(X) \hand{$\geqslant$} 3 (without any normality or unramifiedness hypothesis),
the set of \hand{$\xi$} such that Y\hand{$_\xi$} is geom irred has a complement
of codimension \hand{$\geqslant$}2. We can in any case show that it \unsure{contains} no
hyperplane, \hand{cf below.}

b) The conclusion of 8.16 fails if we drop
the hypothesis prof$_x$X \hand{$\geqslant$} 3 for closed x; for example, it fails for
a nonsingular quadric \var{\inserted[typed]{X}}{X}{insertion}{ega25_25_050-v0008} in P$^3$; \var{\deleted{whose}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_050-v0009} \var{\inserted{indeed all}}{indeed all}{insertion}{ega25_25_050-v0010} the tangent hyperplanes
\var{\xout{form}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_050-v0011} are \var{\deleted{all}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_050-v0012} reducible (in fact, consist of pairs of
intersecting lines), and furthermore form a family of dimension 2,
\var{\margin{\hand{50}}}{\placedmargin{\hand{50}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_050-v0013} hence of codimension 1 in $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ (in fact, the dual of the quadric \var{\deleted{is}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_050-v0014} \var{\deleted{\unsure{a}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_050-v0015}
\tnote{25-050-1}{Remords is rendered second thoughts. The source's est/esst and tout/tous letter doubts remain marked on is and all. The canceled French agreement ending es gives no separate English variant. The noun hyperplanes is retained in the final claim about reducibility, as written in the source.}

}{%

% NOTE: The repeated circle-and-horizontal-line call marks connect the boxed top addition to this position, before Nous allons. They are graphic insertion signs, not textual o---.

\unsure{We} shall now examine more closely the case of surfaces
(the case of curves clearly does not arise from the viewpoint of irreducibility
of hyperplane sections \var{\xout{\unsure{t}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0006}).

(NB I realize with horror that the quadric has no right, in
the use I have made of the word “\unsure{ruled}”, to be called ruled! This must
conflict with our forefathers, and we shall therefore have to invent a more
appropriate word for the notion used here).

\uline{Proposition} 8.18. Suppose \var{\xout{X geom integral}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0007} k alg closed, X \var{\xout{integral and}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0008}
\var{\deleted{and}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_051-v0009} integral (resp. integral and normal)\var{\inserted{of dimension $d \geq 2$ and}}{ of dimension $d \geq 2$ and}{insertion}{ega25_25_051-v0010}
proper over k; let T be a finite closed subset of X such that X-T is
smooth and f $|$X-T is unramified. \unsure{For} the set of \hand{$\xi \in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such
that $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ is \var{\xout{\unsure{es}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0011} geom irred (resp. geom integral) of dimension 1
\unsure{makes} a complement of codimension $\hand{\geq}$ 2, it is n and s that the following
two conditions hold:

a) For every x\hand{$\in$}T, there is a hyperplane section $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ (\hand{$\xi \in$}P(k))
through x, of dimension \var{\over{1}{$d-1$}}{$d-1$}{writing over}{ega25_25_051-v0012}, and irreducible.

b) \var{\inserted[typed]{X'=}}{X'=}{insertion}{ega25_25_051-v0013} X-T is “ruled” (sic) for f, \uline{or} there is a hyperplane
section $Y'_{\hand{\xi}}$ (\hand{$\xi \in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$(k)) of X', \var{\xout{singular}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0014} of dimension \var{\over{1}{d}}{d}{writing over}{ega25_25_051-v0015}-1,
singular and irreducible.

\var{\deleted{Let indeed Z be the union of g($\underline{Y}'^{\mathrm{sing}}$), \var{\xout{and}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0017} the hyperplanes

\uline{smooth} of dimension 1, \var{\xout{\illegible{3} all its points are}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0019}
\var{\xout{\unsure{geom nonsingular} \illegible{5} at the same Y is smooth abov}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0020}
\var{\xout{points of}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_051-v0021} and since it is \var{\inserted{geom}}{geom}{insertion}{ega25_25_051-v0022} connected by 6.1, it is geom irred.}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_051-v0016}

\var{\xout{By 5.6, the set U of P such that Y is geom}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_052-v0001}

Suppose first that X is geom normal. We already know by 8.14 a) that
one can find a closed subset Z' of P of codi\unsure{mension} $\underset{\hand{\rule{0.7em}{0.35pt}}}{>}$2, such that
\hand{$\xi\in$} P - Z' implies that $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ is \uline{separable} over k(\hand{$\xi$}) of dim \var{\inserted{d-}}{d-}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0003}1; for
such a \hand{$\xi$}, it therefore amounts to the same thing for $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ to be geom irred or geom
integral, and the two problems (respé and non respé) examined in
8.18 are therefore equivalent\unsure{.|!} On the other hand, by 5.6, the set
\var{\inserted[typed]{U}}{U}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0004} of \hand{$\xi\in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ such that $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$. is geom integral of dim \var{\over{1}{d}}{d}{writing over}{ega25_25_052-v0005}-1 (the dimension
hypothesis expressing that $\hand{\phi}_{\hand{\xi}}$ is $X_{k(\hand{\xi})}$-regular) is \uline{open}.
We shall exhibit an evident \var{\inserted[typed]{nonempty,}}{nonempty,}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0006} open set P - Z \unsure{contained|contained} in U,
by taking Z to be the union of $\overset{\hand{\rule{3.3em}{0.35pt}}}{g(\underline{Y}'^{\,\mathrm{sing}})}$ and the hyperplanes $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{H}_x$
of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ defined by the f(x), \hand{$x\in$} T. \var{\xout{For}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_052-v0008} For \hand{$\xi\in$} $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ - Z, $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ is \uline{smooth}
of dimension \var{\inserted{d-}}{d-}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0009}1, and since it is geom connected by 6.1, it is geom
integral. We must therefore express \unsure{that|that}\var{\xout{each}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_052-v0010} \var{\inserted[typed]{every}}{every}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0011} irreducible component
of \var{\inserted[typed]{co}}{co}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0012}dimension 1 of Z \var{\xout{does not meet}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_052-v0013}\var{\inserted{meets}}{meets}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0014} the open set U. These irreducible
components are the $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{H}_x$ (possibly repeated, which does not matter), and,
on the other hand, $\overset{\hand{\rule{3.3em}{0.35pt}}}{g(\underline{Y}'^{\,\mathrm{sing}})}$ when the latter does have codim 1, i.e.
X' is “not ruled” for f (NB we use the irreducibility of $\underline{Y}'^{\,\mathrm{sing}}$).
Moreover, for the latter set to meet the open set U,
it is n and s that g($\underline{Y}'^{\,\mathrm{sing}}$) (which contains a dense open subset of it) meets
U, \var{\xout{i.e. that there exists x' X'(k) and a sect}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_052-v0015} This proves 8.18 in this
case. When X is not assumed normal, we apply the preceding result
to the normalization of X; the argument is immediate and I do not give its details
here. \var{\margin{\uline{NB} in the \var{\inserted{\unsure{bis|bas}}}{\unsure{bis|bas}}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0017}respé case, 8.18 is contained in the more precise 8.16 except for d=2. It is for the non respé case that it may be useful not to require d=2, but only $d\geq 2$ ....}}{\placedmargin{\uline{NB} in the \var{\inserted{\unsure{bis|bas}}}{\unsure{bis|bas}}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0017}respé case, 8.18 is contained in the more precise 8.16 except for d=2. It is for the non respé case that it may be useful not to require d=2, but only $d\geq 2$ ....}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_052-v0016}

It remains to make the conditions a) and b) of 8.18 explicit. This leads
us to examine the following situation in general: we assume
X \var{\deleted{integral}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_052-v0018}\var{\inserted{geom irred,}}{geom irred,}{insertion}{ega25_25_052-v0019} over k \var{\deleted{alg closed}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_052-v0020}, and are given a linear subvariety L \hand{of}

\var{\margin{\hand{\unsure{46|48}}}}{\placedmargin{\hand{\unsure{46|48}}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_053-v0001}

$\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ (corresponding to the question of studying the hyperplane sections of X
passing through a given point x, or tangent to X at a given \var{\deleted{simple}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_053-v0002}\var{\inserted{smooth}}{smooth}{insertion}{ega25_25_053-v0003} point),
thus consisting of hyperplanes containing a linear subvariety $L^{o}$ of P
(respectively a point, or the image of a tangent space of X at a smooth point,
in the two cases considered), and we ask whether,
for the generic point \hand{$\xi$} of L (hence for all points of a nonempty
open subset of L), $Y_{\hand{\xi}}$ is \var{\xout{geom integral or at least}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_053-v0005} geom irred, of
dim dim X -1. This is a variant of Bertini, which should appear
in n°\hand{3} and is treated by exactly the same method\var{\inserted[typed]{or, if preferred, reduces to it.}}{ or, if preferred, reduces to it.}{insertion}{ega25_25_053-v0006} The dimension
question is expressed simply by $f(X)\hand{\not\subset} L^{o}$, \var{\xout{There is therefore a}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_053-v0007}
i.e. $X' =$ \var{\xout{X=}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_053-v0008} $f^{-1}(P - L^{o})$ is a dense open subset of X, \var{\xout{and the question}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_053-v0009}
Let \unsure{Q|N} be the projective space of hyperplanes through $L^{o}$ (NB if $L^{o}$ is
defined by a vector subspace $F^{o}$ of E, we have $Q = P($\var{\deleted{E/}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_053-v0010}$F^{o})$) and
consider the canonical morphism (deduced from \var{\xout{E\illegible{1}E/$F^{o}$}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_053-v0011} $F^{o}\hand{\longrightarrow} E$, cf
Chap II)
\[ u :\ P - L^{o} \hand{\longrightarrow} Q, \]
and consider
\[ g = u f' :\quad f^{-1}(P - L^{o}) = X' \hand{\longrightarrow} Q , \]
then \var{\xout{the family of X' \illegible{1} is none oth}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_053-v0012} $L \hand{\simeq} \overset{\hand{\vee}}{Q}$, and the family of
$X'_{\hand{\xi}}$ $(\hand{\xi}\hand{\in} L)$ is none other than the family of “hyperplane sections” relative
to this morphism g. On the other hand, we see immediately that for every
“general” $\hand{\xi}\hand{\in} L$, $X'_{\hand{\xi}}$ is dense in $X_{\hand{\xi}}$, so $X'_{\hand{\xi}}$ is geom irred iff
X is. This being so, the \unsure{theorem|theorem} of Bertini–Zariski shows us that
we have the desired conclusion \var{\xout{dix}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_053-v0013} of irreducibility provided
dim $g(X') \hand{\geqslant} 2$. (Indeed, while we are at it, we could mention a
converse in \hand{3}.1, namely: \var{\xout{Y is ge}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_053-v0014} if X is geom irred, Y is
geom irred iff either dim $f(X)$; $\hand{\neq}$ \unsure{2|1}, or dim $f(X)=1$, and $f(X)$
is contained in a line\hand{D} (defined over $\overset{\hand{\rule{0.6em}{0.35pt}}}{k}$)\var{\inserted{and the generic fiber of $X\to D$ is geom. \unsure{irred}}}{ and the generic fiber of $X\to D$ is geom. \unsure{irred}}{insertion}{ega25_25_053-v0015}\hand{)}; this also allows
\tnote{25-053-1}{The accent correction forme/formé has no separate English effect in consisting. The source's doubled dim, its subsequent X without a subscript, and the semicolon after f(X) are retained. The m/p doubt in théorème remains marked on theorem. The canceled fragment dix is retained without expansion.}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_054}{%
% New English translation from checked cote 25, site 54 (PDF page 55).
% AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).
\scanpage{25/54}
\mspage{\hand{49}}

us, in the present version with L\unsure{,|.}, to have a nec and sufficient condition
\unsure{for|for} geom irreducibility \hand{of $Y_\xi$, $\xi$ gen. in $L$.}

From the cunutesque standpoint, \var{\xout{it}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_054-v0002} and in terms of field
theory, we should express the \var{\xout{(sufficient)}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_054-v0003} condition in terms of
transcendence degree as \unsure{follows|follows}: we choose a “hyperplane
at infinity” containing neither $L^{o}$ nor X, and work in its \unsure{complement|complement},
i.e. essentially on \unsure{the|the} standard affine scheme. We choose
a basis of the \unsure{space|space} of linear forms vanishing on $L^{o}$, \var{\xout{namely}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_054-v0004}
\var{\xout{and consider}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_054-v0005} say $T_{1},\ldots,T_{p}$ (p = codim ($L^{o}$,P)), and consider
their inverse images $t_{1},..,t_{p}$ in the fraction field K of X
(\var{\inserted{X}}{X}{insertion}{ega25_25_054-v0006} assumed integral). At least one of the $t_{i}$, say $t_{1}$, is $\hand{\neq} 0$; let us then
consider $a_{1} = t_{2}/\unsure{t|l}_{1}$,..., $a_{p-1} = t_{p}/t_{1}$. Then dim g(X') is none
other than the transcendence degree of $k(a_{1},..,a_{p-1}) \hand{\subset} K$ over k. Thus
if this transcendence degree is $\hand{\geqslant} 2$, O.K. \var{\xout{Likewise if it}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_054-v0007} If it is 1,
then we must require that over $\overset{\hand{\rule{0.6em}{0.35pt}}}{k}$, f(X) is contained in a linear
subvariety of P containing $L^{o}$ and of dimension one greater\hand{, and that the
generic fiber of $g : X' \to \overline{g(X')}$ is geom. irred.}

Suppose that $L^{o}$ has dimension q; then the fibers of u:
$P - L^{o} \hand{\to} Q$ have dimension q+1, \var{\inserted{hence those of $g$ have dim $\leq q+1$,}}{hence those of $g$ have dim $\leq q+1$,}{insertion}{ega25_25_054-v0008} and consequently we have
\[ \dim g(X') \hand{\geqslant} \dim f(X) - (q+1) , \]
so the dimension condition on g(X') holds provided
\[ \dim f(X) \hand{\geqslant} q+\hand{3} \quad . \]
When q=0, we find the fact mentioned in 8.17 a). \var{\inserted[typed]{\var{\xout{of the}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_054-v0010}}}{\var{\xout{of the}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_054-v0010}}{insertion}{ega25_25_054-v0009} \var{\xout{When q=0,}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_054-v0011}

Returning to the conditions of 8.18, we see that condition a)
concerning an $x \hand{\in} T$ is \unsure{satisfied|satisfied} provided X is not “conical”
at x relative to \unsure{$f$|£}”, in an evident sense.

See whether these last Bertinian developments would fit better
in the next n\textsuperscript{o}, on changing the projective immersion. \var{\margin{\hand{54}}}{\placedmargin{\hand{54}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_054-v0012}
\tnote{25-054-1}{The source's unusual adjective cunutesque is retained without expansion. Its letter doubts in d'/p', façon, complémentaire, le, éspace/espace, and satisfaite remain marked on the corresponding English words. The canceled extra t in suffisante gives no independent English correction. The notation and fiber-dimension claim are retained as written.}

\RegisterPage{ega25_25_063}{%

\scanpage{25/63}
\mspage{58}

appears as a closed subprescheme of $P(\overset{\hand{n}}{\bigwedge}\underline{E})$; in particular, it
is separated over S and quasi-compact over S, and if $\underline{E}$ is of finite type,
it is projective over S. If $\underline{E}$ is of finite \unsure{presentation|presentation}, then
so is $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\underline{E})$: indeed, \var{\xout{\unsure{locally on S,}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_063-v0001} we may
assume S affine, S=Spec(A); then $\underline{E}$ comes from a module of finite type
over a subring of A of finite type, and the formation of $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\underline{E})$
is clearly compatible with base change over S.

When $\underline{E}$ is locally free,\var{\xout{of finite type,}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_063-v0002} then $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\underline{E})$
is \uline{smooth} over S\var{\inserted{\unsure{(with geom. connected fibers.|with geom. connected fibers.}}}{\unsure{(with geom. connected fibers.|with geom. connected fibers.}}{insertion}{ega25_25_063-v0003}. This follows from the more precise fact: if $\underline{E}$ is free,
of rank r, then $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\underline{E})$ can be covered by $\binom{r}{n}$ open subsets
each \var{\inserted[typed]{S-}}{S-}{insertion}{ega25_25_063-v0004}isomorphic to the standard affine space of relative dimension
n(r-n) over S. This decomposition corresponds to the choice, by means of the basis
of $\underline{E}$, of $\binom{r}{n}$ decompositions of $\underline{E}$ into \var{\xout{direct sums}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_063-v0005} exact
sequences
\[
\text{(s)} \qquad 0 \rightarrow \underline{E}' \relbar \underline{E} \relbar \underline{E}'' \longrightarrow 0 \quad ,
\]
with $\underline{E}'$ loc free of rank n. \var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_063-v0006} Such an exact sequence allows
us to define a subfunctor $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\text{s})$ of $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\underline{E})$, restricting ourselves
to the loc free quotients $\underline{G}$ of rank n of $\underline{E}_{S'}$ such that the composite
homomorphism $\underline{E}'_{S'} \longrightarrow \underline{E}_{S'} \longrightarrow \underline{G}$ is surjective (hence bijective). Now
the inclusion $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\text{s}) \rightarrow \underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\underline{E})$ is representable \uline{by open
immersions}, and on the other hand $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\text{s})$ is canonically isomorphic
to the \unsure{vector} bundle \var{\deleted{\unsure{V(E''⊗E')}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_063-v0008} V ( $\underline{\text{Hom}}_{\underline{O}_S}(\underline{E}',\underline{E}'')$).

As a consequence, for example, of this particular structure,
we may point out that if s $\in$ S, then (with $\underline{E}$ loc free of finite rank)
every point of $\underline{\text{Grass}}_n(\underline{E})$ with values in k(s) lifts to a section
over a neighborhood of s. On the other hand, if S=Spec(k), \var{\xout{every}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_063-v0009} k
an infinite field, then every nonempty open subset of $\underline{\text{Grassn}}(\underline{E})$ contains a

point rational over k.

A point of $\underline{\mathrm{Grass}}_n(\underline{E})$ with values in S, i.e. a loc free quotient
Module of rank n \ $\underline{G}$ of $\underline{E}$, canonically defines a subprescheme
of $P(\underline{E})$, namely $P(\underline{G})$. Such a subprescheme\var{\inserted{(but without prescribing the rank of $\underline{G}$)}}{(but without prescribing the rank of $\underline{G}$)}{insertion}{ega25_25_064-v0001} is called a \uline{linear}
% NOTE: The last letter of the French sous-variété is cut by the right edge of the scan.
\uline{subvariety} of $P(\underline{E})$ (relative to S when confusion is possible).
It is therefore a projective bundle, of relative dimension n-1 if $n\geqslant 1$,
(and empty if n=0). We verify immediately that the section of $\underline{\mathrm{Grass}}_n$
$(\underline{E})$, i.e. G, \ is known when we know the corresponding linear subvariety
of $P(\underline{E})$. \unsure{In} this way, \ the Grassmannian can
be interpreted as representing the functor “linear subvarieties
of relative dimension n-1 \var{\xout{\unsure{(resp. empty)}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_064-v0002} of $P_{S'}$” for variable S'
\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_064-v0003}\var{\inserted[typed]{$(n\geqslant 1)$}}{$(n\geqslant 1)$}{insertion}{ega25_25_064-v0004}. It is moreover possible to give an intrinsic characterization
of this last functor, i.e. of the notion of a linear subvariety
\ of relative dimension m: these are the closed subpreschemes
of P, smooth over S and everywhere of relative dimension m, and of
“projective degree 1” \ at every $s\in S$; \ this characterization will
be given in a later Chapter, and we have no need of it here.

Again assuming $\underline{E}$ \ loc free\var{\inserted[typed]{of rank r}}{ of rank r}{insertion}{ega25_25_064-v0005}, let $\underline{E}$ \ be its dual. Then
by polarity we find a canonical isomorphism
\[ \underline{\mathrm{Grass}}_n(E) \hand{\simeq} \underline{\mathrm{Grass}}_{r-n}(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}) \]
associating \ to the quotient \ $\underline{G}$ of $\underline{E}$ \ the quotient \ $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}/\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{G}}$ \ of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}$.
From the viewpoint of linear varieties, the linear variety L of rel dim
m \ in P corresponds to the linear variety\var{\inserted[typed]{, its dual $L^{o}$,}}{, its dual $L^{o}$,}{insertion}{ega25_25_064-v0006} of relative dim
\var{\xout{\unsure{(r-1)-m}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_064-v0007} \ (r-1)-1 -m \ \ of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$,\var{\inserted{i.e. of relative codim m+1 in $\check{P}$}}{i.e. of relative codim m+1 in $\check{P}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_064-v0008} (NB \ r-1 \ is here the common relative dim
of \ P and $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}$ over S), which can be visualized geometrically as follows.
First putting \ n=r-1, we find an isomorphism
\[ \underline{P}(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}) \hand{\simeq} \underline{\mathrm{Grass}}_{r-1}(\underline{E}) \]

\mspage{\hand{\unsure{21}}}

12. \uline{Generalization of earlier results to linear sections}.

Further notation. If $P = P(\underline{E})$, $\underline{E}$ an arbitrary quasi-coherent
Module, we shall also put $\mathrm{Grass}_n(P) = \mathrm{Grass}_{n+1}(\underline{E})$, so that
$\mathrm{Grass}_n(P)$ corresponds to the linear subvarieties of \uline{dimension} n in
P; this holds for $n \geq -1$, with the convention that dim $-1$ means empty
When E is locally free, \var{\xout{\unsure{of rank}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_066-v0001} it is also appropriate to introduce
$\mathrm{Grass}^n(P) = \mathrm{Grass}_{n-1}(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{P}) = \mathrm{Grass}_n(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}})$, which corresponds to the linear
subvarieties of codimension n in P. If $\underline{E}$ has rank r+1, i.e.
P has relative dimension r, we have a canonical isomorphism
$\mathrm{Grass}^n(P) = \mathrm{Grass}_{r-n}(P)$. In what follows we assume E fixed, loc
free of rank r, and we are interested in the linear subvarieties
of P of a given codimension m, hence in $\mathrm{Gr}^m = \mathrm{Gr}^m(P) = \mathrm{Gr}_{m\var{\deleted{\unsure{+1}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_066-v0002}}(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{E})$.
On this prescheme there is therefore a canonic loc free quotient \var{\inserted{$\underline{G}$}}{$\underline{G}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_066-v0003} of rank m
of $\underline{E}_{\mathrm{Gr}}$, say F. \var{\xout{\unsure{The relation di}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_066-v0004} The natural incidence prescheme
on $P\times_S \mathrm{Gr}^m$, which represents the subfunctor of the product functor
corresponding to pairs consisting of a section of $P_{S'}$ and a linear
subvariety of codimension m of $P_{S'}$, \uline{containing} the latter, is then described
explicitly as follows: Let $T=P\times_S \mathrm{Gr}^m$ (or, if preferred, an
arbitrary prescheme over this product); then on \var{\xout{E}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_066-v0005} T we have
$\underline{E}_T$, the quotient $\underline{O}_T(1)$, and the sub-Module that is locally a direct summand
$\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{G}}_T$. Consider the homomorphism obtained by composing the canonical homs
\[ \overset{\hand{\vee}}{G}_T \hand{\longrightarrow} \underline{O}_T(1) \]
which by transposition also corresponds to the analogous composite
homomorphism from the sub-Module $\underline{O}_T(-1)$ of $\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{E}}_T$ to the quotient $\underline{G}_T\mathrm{v}$:
\[ \underline{O}_T(-1) \hand{\longrightarrow} G_T \quad , \]
and can also be regarded as defined by a section of $\underline{G}_T(1)$
\[ \hand{\phi}^m \hand{\in} \hand{\Gamma}(T, \underline{G}_T(1)) \]
\tnote{25-066-1}{The rank r+1 and subsequent rank r, the inserted G followed by say F, and the final v attached to the quotient symbol are retained as written. The source's canoniqu is rendered canonic. The phrase containing the latter retains the source's demonstrative reference.}

The incidence prescheme (resp. its inverse image in T) is then
precisely the preschemes of zeros of either homomorphism,
or again of the section $\hand{\phi}^{m}$. We could denote the incidence
prescheme by $H^{(m)}$; for m=1 we recover that of no.1. If X is
over P, we can put $\underline{Y}^{(m)} = X\times_{P} \underline{H}^{(m)}$, and similarly define
the notation $\underline{Y}^{m}$ when \hand{$\zeta$} is a point of $\unsure{\mathrm{Gr}}^{m}$ with values in an S'.
Thus the \var{\deleted{\unsure{\underline{Y}}}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_067-v0001} $Y^{m}_{\hand{\zeta}}$ are the “\uline{linear} \uline{sections}” of X \var{\inserted[typed]{over P}}{over P}{insertion}{ega25_25_067-v0002} (or rather of
the $X_{S'}$\var{\inserted[typed]{over $P_{S'}$}}{ over $P_{S'}$}{insertion}{ega25_25_067-v0003}) by the linear subvarieties of codimension m of P (or
rather of the $P_{S'}$).

\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_067-v0004}I take this opportunity for a\var{\xout{xx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_067-v0005} self-criticism of notation,
which could appear as early as no.1. It concerns the arbitrary correspondence,
to indicate an object $\underline{Y}$ corresponding to X, of the letter
Y to X (so that if X becomes Z, we no longer quite know what\var{\xout{x}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_067-v0006}
to choose). This drawback has already led me to a few temporary inconsistencies
of notation. Perhaps the slightly more general context
with an integer m as here suggests a reasonable solution: write
$\underline{X}^{(m)}$ in place of $\underline{Y}^{(m)}$, hence $\underline{X}^{(1)}$ in place of the $\underline{Y}$ of no.1. In this
way we would indeed have, apart from a few details ... , $\underline{X}^{(m)(m')} = X^{(m+m')}$\hand{.}
I shall try this notation from now on. Of course, the exponent m
itself invites criticism, for it is common practice in algebraic \unsure{geometry}
to use an exponent to denote the dimension of the varieties
involved. But since we have never used this kind
of convention, I think we keep our hands free in this matter.

We see immediately from the preceding construction of $\underline{X}^{(m)}$ that
there is a canonical\var{\inserted[typed]{P-}}{ P-}{insertion}{ega25_25_067-v0007}isomorphism
\[ X^{(m)} = \underset{1}{\underline{\mathrm{Grass}}}_{m}\var{\xout{\$}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_067-v0008}(\overset{\hand{\vee}}{\underline{F}}) , \]
where $F_{X}$\var{\xout{\unsure{is a}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_067-v0009} ( $\hand{\simeq} f^{*}(\hand{\underline{\Omega}}_{P/S})(1)$) is the kernel of $\underline{E}_{X} \hand{\longrightarrow} O_{X}(1)$\hand{,}
\tnote{25-067-1}{The source's final-letter doubt in géométrie is marked on geometry. I think that essentially all the \unsure{developments} of no.8 starting
with 8.6 can \var{\inserted[typed]{also}}{also}{insertion}{ega25_25_071-v0004} be adapted to the case of linear sections with
arbitrary m. Doing so formally would doubtless be a rather long
and tedious exercise. I must admit that I know of no application
depending essentially on the analysis of this more general
situation, so we are not really obliged to include these
developments in the Éléments. On the other\unsure{x} hand, experience proves that
writing things in a more general setting sometimes forces one to
carry out a better dévissage and gives a better understanding of the whole business, often without much
more trouble; moreover, a certain number of exercises in syntax in a
properly geometric context such as this would do no harm, and
of course it is by no means excluded that we shall need them after all
one day and be glad to find the work done. I therefore leave
the entire decision on this matter to you, and merely sketch the
\var{\xout{\unsure{general}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_071-v0005}statements that might be given on this subject.

Suppose again that $f:X \hand{\longrightarrow} P$ is unramified and X is smooth over
S,\var{\inserted[typed]{with components of dimension \hand{$\geq$} m.}}{with components of dimension \hand{$\geq$} m.}{insertion}{ega25_25_071-v0006}\hand{.} % NOTE: The curved stroke before Dans is the insertion attachment, not a content parenthesis.
