of \unsure{singular|singular} zeros of $\hand{\phi}^m$ relative to Grass$^m$, which also consists
geometrically of pairs $(x,\hand{\xi})$ such that the linear variety $L_{\hand{\xi}}$
meets the tangent space to X at x in excess (regarded as
a linear subvariety of P), i.e. such that the two spaces do not span
all of P. Contrary to what happens for m=1, when
m \var{\inserted[typed]{is arbitrary}}{is arbitrary}{insertion}{ega25_25_071-v0009}\var{\xout{\unsure{2, and X nonempty}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_071-v0010}, the morphism $\underline{X}^{(m)}$ sing $\hand{\longrightarrow}$ X is not in general
smooth, for the variety of L that \var{\inserted{\unsure{pass through x and that}}}{\unsure{pass through x and that} }{insertion}{ega25_25_071-v0011}% NOTE: The curved stroke before coupent is the attachment of the interlinear insertion.
meet a given linear subvariety T\hand{\unsure{$\ni x$}} in excess is not in general
smooth over k: this variety

adt only the closure of the smooth subvariety consisting of the $\hand{\zeta}$ such that
the dimension of $T\hand{\cap}L_{\hand{\zeta}}$ is exactly \uline{one} greater than the
“\unsure{normal|normal}” dimension n-m (n=dim T, m = codim L). Unless I am mistaken, the set
(contained in the relative supersingular set) $V''$ introduced in par
16 (supplements) is precisely the set consisting of pairs
$(x,L_{\hand{\zeta}})$ such that the dimension of $T_x\hand{\cap}L_{\hand{\zeta}}$ is $\hand{\geqslant}$ n-m + 2\hand{)}, so that
\var{\xout{$\underline{X}$}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_072-v0001} $V^1 - V''$ \var{\xout{\unsure{mx}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_072-v0002}is \var{\xout{other than}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_072-v0003} smooth over X, and unless I am mistaken is even \var{\inserted{exactly}}{exactly}{insertion}{ega25_25_072-v0004} the set
of points of $V^1$ smooth over X. (Verification of this point \unsure{would}
require a study of the filtration of the \var{\xout{xx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_072-v0006} Grassmannian \unsure{scheme|scheme} according to the \var{\xout{xxxxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_072-v0007}
dimensions of intersection of the \unsure{variable} L with a fixed T; unless I am mistaken,
we find that the next step of the filtration consists exactly of
the nonsmooth points of the preceding step --- when we define the filtration
\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_072-v0008} not only set-theoretically, but also schematically, using
the lemma on page 16 of the supplements to par.16 \hand{\unsure{)|\S}}. This study would then

\hand{)}. If we similarly define \var{\xout{V(k)}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_072-v0009} $V^{(k)}$ as the subscheme of $X^{(m)}$
corresponding to dim $T_x\hand{\cap}L \hand{\geqslant}$ n-m + k, an immediate calculation gives

\[ \text{dim Grass}^m(P) - \text{dim } V^{(k)} = (k-1)(n-m) + k^2 \]
(at least under the reasonable restrictions k $\hand{\leqslant}$ m, k $\hand{\leqslant}$r-m), barring
an error in the calculation (NB this follows more generally from\var{\xout{xx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_072-v0011} a calculation of
the dimensions of the “cells” occurring in the filtration of Grassmannians
to which I alluded above). For k = 2, we
find a dimension difference $\hand{\geqslant} 4$, so the image of $V''$ in Grass$^m$
has codimension $\hand{\geqslant}$ 4, so if we are interested in what happens outside
sets \var{\inserted[typed]{of Grass$^m$}}{of Grass$^m$}{insertion}{ega25_25_072-v0013} of codimension $\hand{\geqslant}$ 2, we can \var{\xout{xxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_072-v0014} forget $V''$.

is that of the favorable case consider\hand{\unsure{ed}} in the “supplements to par 16”,

relative to the base scheme S: V$^1$- V" is indeed\var{\xout{xx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_073-v0002} smooth over S
(being smooth over X) of relative dimension equal to \uline{one} less than that of
Grass$^m$ over S (as we see by putting k=1 in the formula above).
Thus the results \unsure{of} loc cit apply; in particular we find
that the set of supersingular points of $\hand{\phi}^m$ rel to Grass$^m$
is precisely V" \hand{$\cup$} V$^2$, where V$^2$ is the ramification sub\var{\inserted[typed]{pre}}{pre}{insertion}{ega25_25_073-v0003}scheme
of V$^1$-V" \hand{$\longrightarrow$} G\unsure{r}ass$^m$. Thus we may also say that, outside V",
the supersingular zeros result from the collapse of (at least) two ordinary
singular zeros (but we are not obliged to say so\hand{)}).

\var{\margin{$|$}}{\placedmargin{$|$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_073-v0004} In this way, we essentially have the equivalent of 8.7 a) and b). It \var{\margin{$+$}}{\placedmargin{$+$}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_073-v0005}
should be possible to give an equivalent for 8.7 c), \var{\xout{xxx}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_073-v0006} using
the explicit \var{\xout{structure}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_073-v0007} description of the tangent bundle to Grass$^m$ (analogous to
that in the case m=1); \var{\deleted{and which}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_073-v0008} \var{\inserted{this}}{this}{insertion}{ega25_25_073-v0009} \var{\inserted{also}}{also}{insertion}{ega25_25_073-v0010} implies that for a \var{\inserted[typed]{geometric}}{geometric}{insertion}{ega25_25_073-v0011} point of V$^1$-V" unramified
over Grass$^m$, knowledge of its image in Grass$^m$
implies knowledge of its image in P, provided the first image is a
smooth point of the closed image \var{\inserted[typed]{of}}{of}{insertion}{ega25_25_073-v0012} V$^1$ in Grass$^m$ (we assume S = the spectrum of
a field). I can provide a \var{\xout{slightly}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_073-v0013} more precise statement on request.

Once this point is established, we have obvious corollaries generalizing
8.8, 8.9, 8.11. It is probably just as possible to state
the other propositions of par.8 for arbitrary m. If this requires further
work in writing them up, we can forgo \unsure{this} generalization\hand{,}
\var{\xout{even}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_073-v0014} even if we include the preceding differential developments.
The same holds for the results of n$^o$9.

As for n$^o$10, the situation studied there generalizes to
arbitrary m in the following way. Fix a linear subvariety
C of P of codimension m+1, and consider the projective space Q of linear
\var{\over{\unsure{subvarieties}}{\unsure{subvarieties}}}{\unsure{subvarieties}}{writing over}{ega25_25_073-v0015} L of P of codimension m passing through C. Q is a
\tnote{25-073-1}{The e/g uncertainty in de and the doubtful ending tte in cette are carried by of and this. The doubtful overwritten ariét in sous-variétés is represented by a doubt on the full English word in both branches. The source uses a double quotation mark after V in several places; those marks are retained.}

20.13. \uline{Elementary morphisms and the theorem of} M. \uline{Artin}.

\uline{Definition} 13.1. A morphism $f:X\hand{\to} Y$ of preschemes is called an “\var{\xout{elem}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_075-v0001}
elementary morphism” if X is \var{\xout{a}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_075-v0002} Y-isomorphic to a prescheme of the form
$X' - Z$, where $X'\hand{\to} X$ is a smooth projective morphism with connected geometric
fibers\var{\xout{\unsure{,}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_075-v0003} of dimension 1, and where Z is a closed subprescheme
of X', \var{\xout{étale over}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_075-v0004} such that the morphism $Z\hand{\to} Y$ is étale, surjective, and
of constant degree. A morphism is called \var{\xout{\unsure{multip-elementary}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_075-v0005} poly-elementary
if it is a composite of elementary morphisms. A prescheme X
over a field k is called poly-elementary (over k) if the structural
morphism $X\hand{\to} \mathrm{Spec}(k)$ is poly-elementary.

\uline{Theorem} 13.2. \var{\inserted[typed]{(M.Artin)}}{(M.Artin)}{insertion}{ega25_25_075-v0006} Let X be a \var{\inserted[typed]{geom. irred.}}{geom. irred.}{insertion}{ega25_25_075-v0007} prescheme over a \var{\inserted[typed]{perfect infinite}}{perfect infinite}{insertion}{ega25_25_075-v0008} field k, x a smooth point
of X; then x has a fundamental system of poly-elementary open
neighborhoods.

Replacing X by a given neighborhood of x, \var{\deleted{one \var{\xout{\unsure{can suppo}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_075-v0010} es}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_075-v0009} \var{\inserted{it is}}{it is}{insertion}{ega25_25_075-v0011}
\var{\deleted{reduced to}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_075-v0012} \var{\inserted{equivalent to}}{equivalent to}{insertion}{ega25_25_075-v0013} showing that there exists an elementary open neighborhood of x in X.
\var{\deleted{We may clearly assume X smooth, and}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_075-v0014} \var{\inserted[typed]{\var{\deleted{and affine}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_075-v0016}}}{\var{\deleted{and affine}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_075-v0016}}{insertion}{ega25_25_075-v0015} \var{\over{r}{R}}{R}{writing over}{ega25_25_075-v0017}easoning by induction on
the dimension n of X, we \var{\xout{may assume}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_075-v0018} are reduced to showing \var{\inserted[typed]{that if $n\hand{>}0$,}}{that if $n\hand{>}0$,}{insertion}{ega25_25_075-v0019} \var{\deleted{that}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_075-v0020}there exists
an open neighborhood U of x and an elementary morphism $f: U\hand{\to} V$,
V being a smooth scheme over k (necessarily geom. irred \var{\inserted[typed]{and}}{and}{insertion}{ega25_25_075-v0021} of dim n-1).
\var{\xout{When n=1}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_075-v0022} (The case n=0 is clearly trivial, since then
X is k-isomorphic to Spec(k), which is poly-elementary over Spec(k), it being
understood in 13.1 that we do not exc\hand{l}ude the composite of the empty family of
morphisms --- we should have mentioned this in one form or another in
13.1.) The need to assume k perfect beforehand already appears
in the case n=1: we then take for X' the canonical normal projective model
of the function field K of X (cf Chap II) par.7), the fact that
\tnote{25-075-1}{The defining arrow from X-prime to X is retained exactly as in the checked source. The canceled es remains an incomplete fragment. The French correction of que to qu' before alors changes no English word and has no English variant. The handwritten l in exclut is represented by the corresponding letter in exclude.}

\mspage{71}

\unsure{K|k} is perfect ensures that X' is smooth over k (since X' is in any case
regular), and also ensures that $Z = X' - X$, endowed with the induced reduced
structure, is étale over k. Let us now treat the general case,
in which we may assume $n \hand{\geqslant} 2$.

\var{\xout{Since X is affine and hence quasi-projective,}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_076-v0001} We may clearly assume
X \var{\xout{smooth and}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_076-v0002} affine and hence quasi-\unsure{projective|projective}; then, replacing X
by a projective closure, we may assume X projective, provided
however that we prove that \uline{every} neighborhood of x contains an open
neighborhood U admitting an elementary morphism $U \hand{\to} V$. Similarly, replacing
X by its normalization (finite over X and hence projective, ref.), which does not
change it near x, we may assume X normal, hence, since k is
perfect, geometrically normal over k. \var{\xout{Choose an immersion}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_076-v0003} The
benefit of this hypothesis is that the set Z of points of X at
which X is not smooth over k has codim $\hand{\geqslant} 2$. \var{\xout{\unsure{Let Note that we}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_076-v0004}
Choose a projective immersion $i : X \hand{\longrightarrow} P^r$, \var{\xout{\unsure{We see we can}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_076-v0005}
\var{\xout{\unsure{assume that X is not contained in Then}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_076-v0006} Since we obtain a fundamental
system of neighborhoods of x in $\underline{P}^{\hand{r}}$ by taking sections of the various
$\underline{O}_P(n)$, $n \hand{>} 0$, that do not vanish at x, we conclude that every
neighborhood of x contains a neighborhood of the form X-Y, where Y is a
closed subset of X containing Z, of pure dimension n-1, and such that
$x \hand{\notin} Y$. We shall endow Y with the induced reduced structure, so that
(since k is perfect) the singular set of Y has dimension $\hand{\leqslant}$ n-2. \var{\margin{\unsure{$\nearrow$}}}{\placedmargin{\unsure{$\nearrow$}}}{marginal note placed}{ega25_25_076-v0007}
Enlarging the preceding set Z, we find a closed subset $Z \hand{\subset} Y$ of
dimension $\hand{\leqslant}$ n-2 containing the geom. singular set of X \uline{and} of Y.

The idea of the proof is to fiber X by its intersections
with the linear subvarieties L of P of codimension n-1 containing
\tnote{25-076-1}{The opening K/k uncertainty remains explicit. The live alternatives projectif/prowjectif both give projective in English, with the doubt retained at the corrected source location. The reference ref. and the stated steps of replacing X are left as in the source.}

% NOTE: The complete typed interlinear addition is encoded at its marked insertion point below.

$\underline{Y}^{n-1}_Q$ and $\underline{T}^{n-1}_Q$, \var{\inserted[typed]{\unsure{which|which} are also \var{\inserted[typed]{disjoint}}{disjoint}{insertion}{ega25_25_078-v0002} closed subschemes of $\underline{X}^{n-1}_Q$;}}{\unsure{which|which} are also \var{\inserted[typed]{disjoint}}{disjoint}{insertion}{ega25_25_078-v0002} closed subschemes of $\underline{X}^{n-1}_Q$;}{insertion}{ega25_25_078-v0001} let p,q,r be the structural projections
of these schemes to Q. Then by \var{\deleted{construction}}{}{deletion}{ega25_25_078-v0003}\var{\inserted{\unsure{hypothesis essentially}}}{\unsure{hypothesis essentially}}{insertion}{ega25_25_078-v0004}, p is smooth at the
points over $\hand{\zeta}_o$, and q is étale at the points over $\hand{\zeta}_o$;
the same holds for r, for we see immediately that $\underline{T}^{n-1}_Q$ is precisely
$Tx_kQ$ (a Q-isomorphism). Finally, the morphism p is proper, and since
X is geometrically connected, the fibers of p are geometrically
connected (Bertini's th). Consequently, there exists an open neighborhood
V of $\hand{\zeta}_o$ in X such that \var{\xout{the morphism}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_078-v0005} $\underline{X}^{n-1}_Q|V = X'$ is proper and smooth
over V with geometrically connected fibers, and since the fiber of $\hand{\zeta}_o$,
which is precisely $X\hand{\cap} L_o$, has dimension 1, we may assume that the
fibers of X' over V all have dim. 1. Finally, taking V small enough,
we may assume that $\underline{Y}^{n-1}_Q|V$ and $\underline{T}^{n-1}_Q|V$ are étale over V, so the
sum prescheme Z' of these two (which identifies with a closed sub\var{\xout{pre}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_078-v0006}scheme
of X') is étale over V. Consequently, \var{\xout{in}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_078-v0007} putting $U = X' - Z'$,\var{\xout{\illegible{1}}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_078-v0008}
\var{\xout{And}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_078-v0009} the morphism $U\hand{\to} V$ is an elementary morphism. But \var{\xout{a}}{}{typewriter strike-out}{ega25_25_078-v0010} U is also
an open subset of \var{\inserted[typed]{$X''=$}}{$X''=$}{insertion}{ega25_25_078-v0011}$\underline{X}^{n-1}_Q - \underline{Y}^{n-1}_Q - \underline{\unsure{T|Z}}^{n-1}_Q$, the inverse image of X-Y-T in $\underline{X}^{n-1}_Q$,
and on the other hand $X''\hand{\to}$ X-Y-T is clearly an isomorphism (for
$\underline{X}^{n-1}_Q - \underline{T}^{n-1}_Q \hand{\to}$ X-T is an isomorphism). Hence U identifies with an open subset
of X-Y-T, an open subset moreover containing $\unsure{L|E}_o\hand{\cap} X$ and a fortiori x. This is the
desired neighborhood of x contained in X-Y.
\tnote{25-078-1}{The alternatives qui/qbi both give which, with the doubt retained. The source places the open neighborhood V in X, uses zeta here after xi on the preceding page, and states that U contains the final intersection; all these readings are retained.}

\section*{Text, provenance, and rights}
AI-transcribed by Claude Opus 5.5 (Anthropic, effort high, Message Batches API); checked against the scans, and this edition built, by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode); prompts and records by Claude (Anthropic).

AI-translated by GPT-6 Astra (OpenAI, ChatGPT Pro mode).

Cote 25 is described in the archive as dated ``[à partir de 1967-1987]''. The source of each reading is the attached Montpellier scan. The named published texts---the Grothendieck Circle scan EGA5.pdf, the P. and J. Blass translations (2004), J. C. Reinhold's web re-edition, and the Centre for Grothendieckian Studies item L49d---are not reproduced and have not been consulted for any reading.

The rights in the original work belong to its author or the author's representatives or heirs. Where we hold rights, in the AI-produced transcription, translation, corrections and typesetting, they are dedicated to the public domain under CC0 1.0. This edition is published for educational, non-profit purposes, and we claim no rights we do not hold. If you hold rights in this work and object to its publication here, please open a GitHub issue and we will take it down.

\clearpage\section*{How to read this edition}
The diplomatic form follows archive order. The corrected and reading forms for cote 25 order the material as IV 16, IV 17.15--17.16, IV 20, and IV 21, followed by the letter to Dieudonné. Interleaved leaves are retained with the relevant groups. Cote 24 keeps its archive order; its stray curriculum-vitae leaf at site 76 appears in the diplomatic form only.

Handwriting is blue in the diplomatic form, manuscript deletions red, and typewriter strike-outs gray. Angle brackets mark insertions, and a small rising arrow marks writing over typed text. The corrected form incorporates the additions and replacements and records each difference in the apparatus. The reading form omits the apparatus and its editorial reference marks. Source marginal notes, unresolved readings, and translator queries remain.

The new English translation follows the supplied unified glossary. The mathematical formulas retain the token strings of the mechanically cleaned corrected French text. Translation explanations are confined to translator's notes. The English annotated master preserves the French variant identifiers.

Every scan mark and every uncertainty or illegibility mark links to the corresponding page in the Montpellier viewer. The original page numbers remain distinct from this volume's pagination.
